## Dramatis Personae / 人物表

===Original===
Subtle, the Alchemist

Face, the Housekeeper

Dol Common, their Colleague

Dapper, a Lawyer’s Clerk

Drugger, a Tobacco Man

Lovewit, Master of the House

Sir Epicure Mammon, a Knight

Pertinax Surly, a Gamester

Tribulation Wholesome, a Pastor of Amsterdam

Ananias, a Deacon there

Kastril, the angry Boy

Dame Pliant, his Sister, a Widow

Neighbours

Officers, Attendants, etc.

Scene: London.

===Chinese===
萨特尔（Subtle），炼金术士

费斯（Face），管家

多尔·康门（Dol Common），三人同伙

达珀（Dapper），律师文书

德拉格（Drugger），烟草商

洛维特（Lovewit），房主

伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士（Sir Epicure Mammon），骑士

珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利（Pertinax Surly），赌徒

特里比尤莱申·惠尔萨姆（Tribulation Wholesome），阿姆斯特丹牧师

亚拿尼亚（Ananias），当地执事

卡斯特里尔（Kastril），好斗的少年

普莉恩特夫人（Dame Pliant），其姊，寡妇

邻人们

官差、仆从等

地点：伦敦。

===Original===
*The Alchemist* to the lady most deserving her name and blood: Lady Mary Wroth.

Madam,

===Chinese===
*《炼金术士》*（*The Alchemist*）献给那位最无愧于其门第与血统的贵妇：玛丽·罗思夫人（Lady Mary Wroth）。

夫人：

===Original===
In the age of sacrifices, the truth of religion was not in the greatness and fat of the offerings, but in the devotion and zeal of the sacrificers: else what could a handle of gums have done in the sight of a hecatomb? or how might I appear at this altar, except with those affections that no less love the light and witness, than they have the conscience of your virtue? If what I offer bear an acceptable odour, and hold the first strength, it is your value of it, which remembers where, when, and to whom it was kindled. Otherwise, as the times are, there comes rarely forth that thing so full of authority or example, but by assiduity and custom grows less, and loses. This, yet, safe in your judgment (which is a Sidney’s) is forbidden to speak more, lest it talk or look like one of the ambitious faces of the time, who, the more they paint, are the less themselves.

===Chinese===
在献祭的时代，宗教的真谛不在祭品的丰隆肥腴，而在献祭者的虔诚与热忱：否则，一把树脂香料在百牛大祭（hecatomb）面前又算得了什么？我又焉敢走近这座祭坛，若非怀着这样一种深情——它既深念夫人美德在心，也同样热爱光天化日与世人见证？倘若我所奉献的这祭品气味可悦，首香未散，那是夫人对它的珍视使然——正是这份珍视，记得它是在何处、何时、为谁点燃。不然的话，时风如此，世间难得再有那样充满权威与典范的东西问世，纵然出了，也终因频频搬演、习以为常而渐减渐失。而这一部，既已安然托庇于夫人的品鉴（那是锡德尼家（Sidney）的品鉴），便不容它再多说一句，唯恐它言语举止像极了时下那些涂脂抹粉的野心面孔——粉涂得越厚，便越不成本来的自己。

===Original===
Your ladyship’s true honourer,

Ben Jonson

===Chinese===
夫人最忠诚的敬慕者，

本·琼森（Ben Jonson）

## To the Reader / 致读者

===Original===
If thou beest more, thou art an understander, and then I trust thee. If thou art one that takest up, and but a pretender, beware of what hands thou receivest thy commodity; for thou wert never more fair in the way to be cozened, than in this age, in poetry, especially in plays: wherein, now the concupiscence of dances and of antics so reigneth, as to run away from nature, and be afraid of her, is the only point of art that tickles the spectators. But how out of purpose, and place, do I name art? When the professors are grown so obstinate contemners of it, and presumers on their own naturals, as they are deriders of all diligence that way, and, by simple mocking at the terms, when they understand not the things, think to get off wittily with their ignorance. Nay, they are esteemed the more learned, and sufficient for this, by the many, through their excellent vice of judgment. For they commend writers, as they do fencers or wrestlers; who if they come in robustuously, and put for it with a great deal of violence, are received for the braver fellows: when many times their own rudeness is the cause of their disgrace, and a little touch of their adversary gives all that boisterous force the foil. I deny not, but that these men, who always seek to do more than enough, may some time happen on some thing that is good, and great; but very seldom; and when it comes it doth not recompense the rest of their ill. It sticks out, perhaps, and is more eminent, because all is sordid and vile about it: as lights are more discerned in a thick darkness, than a faint shadow. I speak not this, out of a hope to do good to any man against his will; for I know, if it were put to the question of theirs and mine, the worse would find more suffrages: because the most favour common errors. But I give thee this warning, that there is a great difference between those, that, to gain the opinion of copy, utter all they can, however unfitly; and those that use election and a mean. For it is only the disease of the unskilful, to think rude things greater than polished; or scattered more numerous than composed.

===Chinese===
你若不止于此，你便是个真懂的读者，那我就信得过你。你若只是个赊账买货、假充内行的人，那就当心你从什么人手里接货；因为在这个时代，在诗——尤其在戏剧上，你正走在最容易被骗的一条大路上：如今舞蹈与滑稽把戏的淫靡当道，以致「逃离自然、惧怕自然」竟成了逗乐看客的唯一艺术要诀。可我多么不合时宜、文不对题地提起「艺术」二字！它的教授们早已变成艺术顽固的蔑视者、自身天资的狂妄自恃者，对那一门里的一切勤勉都加以讥嘲，而且明明不懂那些事物，却单会取笑那些名目，自以为凭着无知便能俏皮地蒙混过关。不，在众人眼里，他们反倒因此更显得博学、更能胜任——全亏他们那出色的判断力痼疾。因为他们夸奖作家，就像夸奖击剑师或角力士：谁上场来势汹汹、拼死蛮干，谁就被当作更勇敢的好汉：殊不知多少回他们自己的粗鲁正是出丑的根由，对手轻轻一触，便叫那一身横暴的力气全数落空。我不否认，这些一味求多、总想做过头的人，偶尔也会碰上一样好的、了不起的东西；但极少；而且即使碰上了，也抵不上其余种种的坏。那东西或许会凸出来、显得出众，只因它四周一切都卑污下贱：正如灯火在浓黑之中，比在淡淡的阴影里更显眼。我说这些，并非指望违背谁的本意去让他受益；我知道，倘若把我这一套和他们的那一套交付公断，坏的一方准会得到更多的选票：因为多数人偏爱众人都犯的错误。但我给你这个警告：有一种人，为了博得「丰盛」的名声，把肚里的货色倾囊倒出，不管合不合宜；另一种人则讲求抉择与节制；这两者之间大有分别。因为只有外行的痼疾，才会认为粗野的东西高于打磨过的，散漫芜杂的东西多于凝练结撰的。

## Argument / 本事

===Original===
The sickness hot, a master quit, for fear, His house in town, and left one servant there; Ease him corrupted, and gave means to know

A Cheater, and his punk; who now brought low, Leaving their narrow practice, were become Cozeners at large; and only wanting some House to set up, with him they here contract, Each for a share, and all begin to act. Much company they draw, and much abuse, In casting figures, telling fortunes, news, Selling of flies, flat bawdry with the stone, Till it, and they, and all in fume are gone.

===Chinese===
瘟疫正炽，主人畏祸离城，撇下城中宅子，只留一仆看守；安逸腐蚀了这仆人，使他得以结识

一个骗子，和骗子同居的娼妇；这两人本已落魄，弃了小打小闹的营生，正想改做大局；只缺一处开张的宅院，便与他当场立约，各占一股，三人同台开演。主顾盈门，骗术百出：排盘起数、算命、卖新闻，兜售灵蝇（flies，侍灵小鬼），借石头公然拉皮条，直到石头、骗子和这一切随烟散尽。

## Prologue / 开场诗

===Original===
Fortune, that favours fools, these two short hours, We wish away, both for your sakes and ours, Judging spectators; and desire, in place, To the author justice, to ourselves but grace. Our scene is London, ’cause we would make known, No country’s mirth is better than our own: No clime breeds better matter for your whore, Bawd, squire, impostor, many persons more, Whose manners, now called humours, feed the stage; And which have still been subject for the rage Or spleen of comic writers. Though this pen Did never aim to grieve, but better men; Howe’er the age he lives in doth endure The vices that she breeds, above their cure. But when the wholesome remedies are sweet, And in their working gain and profit meet, He hopes to find no spirit so much diseased, But will with such fair correctives be pleased: For here he doth not fear who can apply. If there be any that will sit so nigh Unto the stream, to look what it doth run, They shall find things, they’d think or wish were done; They are so natural follies, but so shown, As even the doers may see, and yet not own.

===Chinese===
福耳图那偏爱愚人；这短短两小时，我们既为您、也为我们自己，愿把她打发走，明察的看客们；我们在此但求：还作者以公道，给我们自己只求开恩。我们的场景是伦敦，因为我们要叫人知道：没有哪国的欢乐胜过本国；没有哪方水土，能滋养出更好的素材，供您的娼妓、鸨母、纨绔、骗子，以及更多的各色人等之用——他们的风习，如今唤作「癖性」（humours），正是戏台的食粮；也向来是喜剧作家怒火与脾囊施展的对象。虽然这支笔从无意刺伤，要伤也只伤更坏的人；然而无论他身处的时代，如何纵容它自己生养、且已病入膏肓的种种恶习。但既然祛病的药剂甘甜，服下去又见效又得利，他希望世上找不到一个病得那样重的灵魂，竟会不欢喜这样体面的矫正之药：因为在此他不怕有谁真能对号入座。倘有人偏要坐得离这溪流那样近，看它究竟流淌些什么，他们自会看见一些自己想过或盼过的事；这些全是天生的蠢行，只是如此搬演出来，连行事者本人都看得见，却认不出是自家。

## Act I / 第一幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
A room in **Lovewit’s** house.

**Face**：Believ’t, I will.

**Subtle**：Thy worst. I fart at thee.

**Dol Common**：Have you your wits? Why, gentlemen! For love—

**Face**：Sirrah, I’ll strip you—

**Subtle**：What to do? Lick figs Out at my—

**Face**：Rogue, rogue!—out of all your sleights.

**Dol Common**：Nay, look ye, sovereign, general, are you madmen?

**Subtle**：O, let the wild sheep loose. I’ll gum your silks With good strong water, an you come.

**Dol Common**：Will you have The neighbours hear you? Will you betray all? Hark! I hear somebody.

**Face**：Sirrah—

**Subtle**：I shall mar All that the tailor has made, if you approach.

**Face**：You most notorious whelp, you insolent slave, Dare you do this?

**Subtle**：Yes, faith; yes, faith.

**Face**：Why, who Am I, my mongrel? Who am I?

===Chinese===
**洛维特**宅中的一间屋子。

**费斯**：信我的话，我一定干。

**萨特尔**：随你把最狠的使出来。我冲你放个屁。

**多尔·康门**：你们脑子还在吗？咳，二位爷！看在情分——

**费斯**：小子，我要剥你的皮——

**萨特尔**：剥了做什么？舔无花果，舔我的——

**费斯**：流氓，流氓！——把你那套把戏统统剥个精光。

**多尔·康门**：得了，瞧瞧你们，一个「至尊」，一个「大将军」，你们是疯子不成？

**萨特尔**：哦，把野羊放出来吧。你敢过来，我就拿烈性强水把你那身丝绸胶作一团。

**多尔·康门**：你们要叫邻居听见吗？要全盘泄底吗？听！有人来了。

**费斯**：小子——

**萨特尔**：你要敢逼近，我把裁缝给你做的一身全毁了。

**费斯**：你这大名鼎鼎的崽子，你这无法无天的奴才，敢来这一套？

**萨特尔**：敢，真的；真敢。

**费斯**：那么，我倒是谁，我的杂种？我是谁？

===Original===
**Subtle**：I’ll tell you, Since you know not yourself.

**Face**：Speak lower, rogue.

**Subtle**：Yes, you were once (time’s not long past) the good, Honest, plain, livery-three-pound-thrum, that kept Your master’s worship’s house here in the Friars, For the vacations—

**Face**：Will you be so loud?

**Subtle**：Since, by my means, translated Suburb-Captain.

**Face**：By your means, Doctor Dog!

**Subtle**：Within man’s memory, All this I speak of.

**Face**：Why, I pray you, have I Been countenanced by you, or you by me? Do but collect, sir, where I met you first.

**Subtle**：I do not hear well.

**Face**：Not of this, I think it. But I shall put you in mind, sir;—at Pie-corner, Taking your meal of steam in, from cooks’ stalls, Where, like the father of hunger, you did walk Piteously costive, with your pinched-horn-nose, And your complexion of the Roman wash, Stuck full of black and melancholic worms, Like powder corns shot at the artillery-yard.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：我告诉你，既然你自己都不知道自己是谁。

**费斯**：说小声点，恶棍。

**萨特尔**：好吧，你从前（时日不久）不过是个规规矩矩、老实巴交、年俸三镑、穿号衣的粗呢邋遢跟班，替你主人大人在修士区看守着这座宅子，趁着主人歇夏下乡——

**费斯**：你要嚷得这么响？

**萨特尔**：后来靠我的提携，才擢升作了郊区队长。

**费斯**：靠你的提携，狗博士！

**萨特尔**：都在活人的记忆之内，我说的这些事。

**费斯**：哦，那么请问，是我抬举过你，还是你抬举过我？先生，你只消想一想，咱俩头一回是在哪儿碰上的。

**萨特尔**：我耳朵背。

**费斯**：这一桩，我看不背吧。可是我提醒你一句，先生；——在派角（Pie Corner），你对着厨子摊子吸蒸汽当饭吃呢，像个饿神爷在那儿踱步，可怜巴巴地便秘着，捏着一只挤角尖鼻，一张脸跟罗马墙水刷过似的，密密麻麻长满黑色忧郁的蛆虫，活像炮兵场里打出去的火药籽。

===Original===
**Subtle**：I wish you could advance your voice a little.

**Face**：When you went pinned up in the several rags You had raked and picked from dunghills, before day; Your feet in mouldy slippers, for your kibes; A felt of rug, and a thin threaden cloak, That scarce would cover your no buttocks—

**Subtle**：So, sir!

**Face**：When all your alchemy, and your algebra, Your minerals, vegetals, and animals, Your conjuring, cozening, and your dozen of trades, Could not relieve your corps with so much linen Would make you tinder, but to see a fire; I gave you countenance, credit for your coals, Your stills, your glasses, your materials; Built you a furnace, drew you customers, Advanced all your black arts; lent you, beside, A house to practise in—

**Subtle**：Your master’s house!

**Face**：Where you have studied the more thriving skill Of bawdry since.

**Subtle**：Yes, in your master’s house. You and the rats here kept possession. Make it not strange. I know you were one could keep The buttery-hatch still locked, and save the chippings, Sell the dole beer to aqua-vitae men, The which, together with your Christmas vails At post-and-pair, your letting out of counters, Made you a pretty stock, some twenty marks, And gave you credit to converse with cobwebs, Here, since your mistress’ death hath broke up house.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：但愿你能把嗓门再抬高一点。

**费斯**：那时候你浑身别别扭扭裹着各色破布条，都是天亮前从粪堆上扒来拣来的；脚上一双霉烂的拖鞋，护着你的冻疮；头顶一方粗毡，身上一件线纱薄斗篷，遮不住你那没有屁股的屁股——

**萨特尔**：好嘛，先生！

**费斯**：那时候你全部的炼金术、全部的代数学，你的矿物、植物、动物，你的召魂、行骗，加上你那一打行当，都换不来那么一丁点麻布给你那具尸身做衬衣，见火就着；是我给了你体面，赊账供你煤炭、你的蒸馏罐、你的玻璃瓶、你的各色材料；给你砌了一座炉子，给你招徕主顾，把你那些黑功夫一样样抬举起来；此外还借给你——一座开张营业的宅子——

**萨特尔**：你主人的宅子！

**费斯**：你就在那儿进修了更发财的手艺——拉皮条。

**萨特尔**：不错，就在你主人的宅子里。你跟耗子们在这儿占着窝呢。少装糊涂。我知道你这种人，能把酒食间的小窗一直锁得死死的，攒下碎面饼，把赈济麦酒转卖给烧酒贩子，这些进项，加上你玩牌戏得的圣诞节赏钱，加上你出租柜台桌凳，给你攒下一注好本钱，足有二十来马克，自打你女主人归天、这宅子封了门，你就有本钱在这儿跟蜘蛛网谈心了。

===Original===
**Face**：You might talk softlier, rascal.

**Subtle**：No, you scarab, I’ll thunder you in pieces: I will teach you How to beware to tempt a Fury again, That carries tempest in his hand and voice.

**Face**：The place has made you valiant.

**Subtle**：No, your clothes.— Thou vermin, have I ta’en thee out of dung, So poor, so wretched, when no living thing Would keep thee company, but a spider, or worse? Raised thee from brooms, and dust, and watering-pots, Sublimed thee, and exalted thee, and fixed thee In the third region, called our state of grace? Wrought thee to spirit, to quintessence, with pains Would twice have won me the philosopher’s work? Put thee in words and fashion, made thee fit For more than ordinary fellowships? Given thee thy oaths, thy quarrelling dimensions, Thy rules to cheat at horse-race, cock-pit, cards, Dice, or whatever gallant tincture else? Made thee a second in mine own great art? And have I this for thanks! Do you rebel, Do you fly out in the projection? Would you be gone now?

**Dol Common**：Gentlemen, what mean you? Will you mar all?

**Subtle**：Slave, thou hadst had no name—

**Dol Common**：Will you undo yourselves with civil war?

**Subtle**：Never been known, past *equi clibanum*, The heat of horse-dung, under ground, in cellars, Or an alehouse darker than deaf John’s; been lost To all mankind, but laundresses and tapsters, Had not I been.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：你说话放轻点儿，混账。

**萨特尔**：偏不，你这屎壳郎，我要把你雷成碎片：我要教教你，下回再敢招惹复仇女神——那手心里、嗓门里都带着风暴的人物——是什么下场。

**费斯**：这地方壮了你的胆。

**萨特尔**：不，是你这身衣裳壮的。——你这虫豸，是我把你从粪堆里捡出来的，那样穷，那样贱，那时候没一个活物肯跟你作伴，只有蜘蛛，或者更次的玩意儿？是我把你从扫帚、灰尘和洒水壶里提拔起来，把你升华，把你提纯，把你固定在我们唤作恩典状态的第三重天上？是我千辛万苦把你炼成精灵、炼成精华，下的苦功够我赢两回哲人石？是我调教出你的言谈举止，把你拾掇得配得上比寻常更高的交游？把你的赌咒、你吵架的尺寸给了你，教你在赛马场、斗鸡场、纸牌、骰子，以及其他一切花哨名目上作弊的规矩？把你收作我本人这门伟大艺术的第二把手？我落的就是这个报答！你造反了？你要在投射（点金）里爆飞出去？你这就要走？

**多尔·康门**：二位爷，这是要干什么？要把全盘毁了？

**萨特尔**：奴才，你本来连个名号都不配有——

**多尔·康门**：你们非要打场内战、自取灭亡不可？

**萨特尔**：你根本不会有人知道你过了 *equi clibanum*（马炉之热）那一关，没在地窖地下、马粪的热气里熬过，没在比聋子约翰家还黑的酒馆里蹲过；要不是有我，你早就从人类记忆里失踪了，记得你的只除了洗衣婆和酒保。

===Original===
**Dol Common**：Do you know who hears you, Sovereign?

**Face**：Sirrah—

**Dol Common**：Nay, General, I thought you were civil.

**Face**：I shall turn desperate, if you grow thus loud.

**Subtle**：And hang thyself, I care not.

**Face**：Hang thee, collier, And all thy pots, and pans, in picture, I will, Since thou hast moved me—

**Dol Common**：O, this will o’erthrow all.

**Face**：Write thee up bawd in Paul’s, have all thy tricks Of cozening with a hollow coal, dust, scrapings, Searching for things lost, with a sieve and sheers, Erecting figures in your rows of houses, And taking in of shadows with a glass, Told in red letters; and a face cut for thee, Worse than Gamaliel Ratsey’s.

**Dol Common**：Are you sound? Have you your senses, masters?

**Face**：I will have A book, but barely reckoning thy impostures, Shall prove a true philosopher’s stone to printers.

**Subtle**：Away, you trencher-rascal!

**Face**：Out, you dog-leech! The vomit of all prisons—

**Dol Common**：Will you be Your own destructions, gentlemen?

**Face**：Still spewed out For lying too heavy on the basket.

**Subtle**：Cheater!

**Face**：Bawd!

**Subtle**：Cowherd!

**Face**：Conjurer!

**Subtle**：Cutpurse!

**Face**：Witch!

===Chinese===
**多尔·康门**：你们可知道谁在听着，至尊？

**费斯**：小子——

**多尔·康门**：得了，大将军，我还当你是懂规矩的人。

**费斯**：你再这么嚷，我可要豁出命来了。

**萨特尔**：那你去上吊，我不拦着。

**费斯**：上吊的是你，烧炭的，连你那一锅一罐，画在画上，我都会——既然你惹了我——

**多尔·康门**：哎呀，这可要全砸了。

**费斯**：到圣保罗教堂去贴你的大字，写你是老鸨，把你那套把戏全登出来：空心煤、香灰、地皮搓屑装神弄鬼，拿一面筛子一把剪刀寻找失物，在成排的宅子里竖星盘，拿一面玻璃镜收摄影子，全用红字母印出来；再给你刻一幅嘴脸，比加米利·拉特西（Gamaliel Ratsey）的还狰狞。

**多尔·康门**：你们没毛病吧？还有知觉吗，二位师傅？

**费斯**：我要写一本书，单只数一数你这些坑蒙拐骗，就够给印书人当真哲人石使。

**萨特尔**：去你的，你个吃盘子的恶棍！

**费斯**：滚你的，你个狗医生的狗皮膏！各监狱吐出来的呕吐物——

**多尔·康门**：二位爷，你们非要自我毁灭不可？

**费斯**：回回都被吐出来——只因躺在刑筐上压得太沉。

**萨特尔**：骗子！

**费斯**：老鸨！

**萨特尔**：放牛的！

**费斯**：妖道！

**萨特尔**：扒手！

**费斯**：巫婆！

===Original===
**Dol Common**：O me! We are ruined, lost! Have you no more regard To your reputations? Where’s your judgment? ’Slight, Have yet some care of me, of your republic—

**Face**：Away, this brach! I’ll bring thee, rogue, within The statute of sorcery, *tricesimo tertio* Of Harry the Eighth: ay, and perhaps thy neck Within a noose, for laundering gold and barbing it.

**Dol Common**：*（Snatches **Face’s** sword.）* You’ll bring your head within a cockscomb, will you? And you, sir, with your menstrue— *（Dashes **Subtle’s** vial out of his hand.）* Gather it up.— ’Sdeath, you abominable pair of stinkards, Leave off your barking, and grow one again, Or, by the light that shines, I’ll cut your throats. I’ll not be made a prey unto the marshal, For ne’er a snarling dog-bolt of you both. Have you together cozened all this while, And all the world, and shall it now be said, You’ve made most courteous shift to cozen yourselves? *（To **Face**.）* You will accuse him! You will “bring him in Within the statute!” Who shall take your word? A whoreson, upstart, apocryphal Captain, Whom not a Puritan in Blackfriars will trust So much as for a feather: *（To **Subtle**.）* and you, too, Will give the cause, forsooth! You will insult, And claim a primacy in the divisions! You must be chief! As if you only had The powder to project with, and the work Were not begun out of equality? The venture tripartite? All things in common? Without priority? ’Sdeath! You perpetual curs, Fall to your couples again, and cozen kindly, And heartily, and lovingly, as you should, And lose not the beginning of a term, Or, by this hand, I shall grow factious too, And take my part, and quit you.

===Chinese===
**多尔·康门**：哎呀我的天！咱们完蛋了，毁了！你们一点也不顾惜名声了吗？脑子哪儿去了？『上帝的』，也给我留几分体面，顾顾咱们这个共和邦——

**费斯**：去你的，母猎狗！我要把这流氓你，送上巫术法令，亨利八世法令的 *tricesimo tertio*（第三十三条）：哼，说不定还要把你这脖子套进绞索，谁叫你洗金子、给金子修边儿。

**多尔·康门**：*（一把抢下**费斯**的剑。）*你倒想把脑袋套进愚人帽，是不是？还有你，先生，抱着你的溶剂不放—— *（把**萨特尔**手里的药瓶打落。）*自己拣去吧。——『天杀的』，你们这对臭气熏天的宝贝，别再汪汪对咬了，重新合成一体；不然，凭着头顶照着的亮光，我割了你们的喉咙。我才不肯让警长把我也当赃物收了去，为了你们这两条谁也不稀罕的龇牙赖皮狗。你们合伙行骗这么久，骗遍了全世界，如今倒要叫人说，你们客客气气地演了出互相行骗的好戏？*（向**费斯**。）*你要告发他！你要“把他送进法令里去”！谁信你的话？一个婊子养的、暴发户、伪托诏书的队长，黑衣修士区连一个清教徒都不肯信他，连一根羽毛钱都不敢赊给他：*（向**萨特尔**。）*还有你，也要逞能，说穿了！也要摆威风，要在分赃上争首席！你非要当头儿不可！倒像只有你一个拿着可以投射的药粉，这事业不是从平等起头的？不是三股合伙的营生？不是一切共有？没有先后？『天杀的』，你们这对万年赖皮狗，赶紧各自归位，重修旧好，客客气气、亲亲热热、有情有义地骗下去，别耽误了这个开庭季的开张；不然，凭我这只手，我也要拉帮结派，自己站到一边去，跟你们算账。

===Original===
**Face**：'Tis his fault; He ever murmurs, and objects his pains, And says, the weight of all lies upon him.

**Subtle**：Why, so it does.

**Dol Common**：How does it? Do not we Sustain our parts?

**Subtle**：Yes, but they are not equal.

**Dol Common**：Why, if your part exceed today, I hope Ours may, tomorrow match it.

**Subtle**：Ay, they *may*.

**Dol Common**：May, murmuring mastiff! Ay, and do. Death on me! Help me to throttle him.

**Subtle**：Dorothy! Mistress Dorothy! 'Ods precious, I'll do anything. What do you mean?

**Dol Common**：Because o' your fermentation and cibation?

**Subtle**：Not I, by heaven—

===Chinese===
**费斯**：都是他的错；他老在嘀咕，老念叨自己的辛苦，说千斤重担全压在他一个人身上。

**萨特尔**：可不是，本来就在我身上。

**多尔·康门**：怎么在您身上？难道我们没各自担着各自的一份？

**萨特尔**：担是担着，就是不一样重。

**多尔·康门**：哼，就算您今天多担了些，但愿我们明天也能撵上。

**萨特尔**：是啊，「也许」能。

**多尔·康门**：也许，嘀咕个没完的大獒犬！是啊，还做得到。气死我了！帮我掐死他。

**萨特尔**：多萝西！多萝西小姐！『老天爷的』，我什么都依你。你要怎么样？

**多尔·康门**：就为着您的发酵（fermentation）和添料（cibation）？

**萨特尔**：不是我，皇天在上——

===Original===
**Dol Common**：Your Sol and Luna *（To **Face**.）* —help me.

**Subtle**：Would I were hanged then? I'll conform myself.

**Dol Common**：Will you, sir? Do so then, and quickly: swear.

**Subtle**：What should I swear?

**Dol Common**：To leave your faction, sir, And labour kindly in the common work.

**Subtle**：Let me not breathe if I meant aught beside. I only used those speeches as a spur To him.

**Dol Common**：I hope we need no spurs, sir. Do we?

**Face**：'Slid, prove today, who shall shark best.

**Subtle**：Agreed.

**Dol Common**：Yes, and work close and friendly.

**Subtle**：'Slight, the knot Shall grow the stronger for this breach, with me.

===Chinese===
**多尔·康门**：你们家的「日」（Sol）「月」（Luna）*（向**费斯**。）*——帮我一把。

**萨特尔**：那还不把我吊死算了？我服软，我服软。

**多尔·康门**：当真，先生？那就快些服软：起誓。

**萨特尔**：叫我起什么誓？

**多尔·康门**：誓不再结您的帮派，先生，从此和和气气出力干公中的活计。

**萨特尔**：叫我喘不上气，要是我还有半点别的心思。我那些话，不过是拿它当根马刺，刺他一刺。

**多尔·康门**：我盼着咱们如今用不着马刺了，先生。是不是？

**费斯**：『圣母的』，今天就比一比，看谁宰肥羊宰得漂亮。

**萨特尔**：一言为定。

**多尔·康门**：对，还要咬得紧、处得亲热。

**萨特尔**：『上帝的』，这一场别扭过去，这个结只会在我的心上系得更牢。

===Original===
**Dol Common**：Why, so, my good baboons! Shall we go make A sort of sober, scurvy, precise neighbours, That scarce have smiled twice since the king came in, A feast of laughter at our follies? Rascals, Would run themselves from breath, to see me ride, Or you t' have but a hole to thrust your heads in, For which you should pay ear-rent? No, agree. And may Don Provost ride a feasting long, In his old velvet jerkin and stained scarfs, My noble Sovereign, and worthy General, Ere we contribute a new crewel garter To his most worsted worship.

===Chinese===
**多尔·康门**：这才像话，我的好狒狒们！难道咱们要去逗笑那一帮道貌岸然、蹩脚促狭、一丝不苟的街坊——那帮自打国王登基以来统共没笑过两回的邻居——拿咱们的荒唐给他们当一顿大笑的宴席吗？那帮恶棍，巴不得跑断了气来看我骑马游街，或者看你把脑袋塞进木枷的窟窿里，还得为那窟窿付「耳朵租金」？不，讲和吧。但愿「堂·普罗沃斯特」（Don Provost）还照旧穿着那件旧天鹅绒紧身衣、扎着油渍斑斑的围巾，长年赶他的酒宴——我尊贵的至尊、得力的大将军——总强过咱们再凑一条新「绒线」（crewel，谐「狠心」）袜带，进贡给他那位「精纺」（worsted，谐「一败涂地」）大人。

===Original===
**Subtle**：Royal Dol! Spoken like Claridiana, and thyself.

**Face**：For which at supper, thou shalt sit in triumph, And not be styled Dol Common, but Dol Proper, Dol Singular: the longest cut at night, Shall draw thee for his Dol Particular.

**Subtle**：Who's that? One rings. To the window, Dol:

**Face**：O, fear not him. While there dies one a week O' the plague, he's safe, from thinking toward London. Beside, he's busy at his hop-yards now; I had a letter from him. If he do, He'll send such word, for airing of the house, As you shall have sufficient time to quit it: Though we break up a fortnight, 'tis no matter.

**Subtle**：Who is it, Dol?

**Dol Common**：A fine young quodling.

**Face**：O, My lawyer's clerk, I lighted on last night, In Holborn, at the Dagger. He would have (I told you of him) a familiar, To rifle with at horses, and win cups.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：高贵的多尔！说得像克拉里迪安娜（Claridiana）转世，又是你本人的口吻。

**费斯**：因此今晚用饭，你要端坐凯旋席上；那时再没人叫你「多尔·康门」（庸常的多尔），要叫「多尔·普罗珀」（专属的多尔）、「多尔·辛古拉」（独一无二的多尔）：夜里切肉，最长的一刀切下去，切中的那位就得认你作他的「多尔·帕蒂库拉」（私藏的多尔）。

**萨特尔**：谁来了？有人拉铃。到窗边去，多尔：

**费斯**：哦，不用怕他。只要瘟疫一礼拜还死得上一两个人，他就安安稳稳，绝不会起念头往伦敦来。再说，他眼下正忙着侍弄他的啤酒花田；我接到过他一封信。他真要回来，也会先打招呼，说要给宅子通风透气，好教你们有充裕的工夫撤退：哪怕咱们歇业半个月，也不打紧。

**萨特尔**：是谁，多尔？

**多尔·康门**：一个俊俏的小乖仔。

**费斯**：哦，是我昨晚在霍尔本（Holborn）「匕首」客栈（the Dagger）撞见的那个律师文书。我跟你说过的——他想要一个侍灵，好在赌马时帮他捞钱、赢杯盏。

===Original===
**Dol Common**：O, let him in.

**Subtle**：Stay. Who shall do't?

**Face**：Get you Your robes on: I will meet him as going out.

**Dol Common**：And what shall I do?

**Face**：Not be seen; away!

**Subtle**：Enough.

===Chinese===
**多尔·康门**：哦，放他进来吧。

**萨特尔**：慢着。谁去开门？

**费斯**：你去把法袍穿上：我装作正要出门，去迎他。

**多尔·康门**：那我干什么？

**费斯**：别露面；走开！

**萨特尔**：就这么办。

===Original===
**Face**：*（Aloud and retiring.）* God be wi' you, sir, I pray you let him know that I was here: His name is Dapper. I would gladly have stayed, but—

**Dapper**：*（Within.）* Captain, I am here.

**Face**：Who's that?—He's come, I think, Doctor.

**Dapper**：In truth I am very sorry, Captain.

**Face**：But I thought Sure I should meet you.

**Dapper**：Ay, I am very glad. I had a scurvy writ or two to make, And I had lent my watch last night to one That dines today at the sheriff's, and so was robbed Of my past-time.

**Face**：This is his worship.

**Dapper**：Is he a Doctor?

**Face**：Yes.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：*（高声，一面退场。）*再见，先生；求您转告他我来过：他叫达珀。我本想多待一会儿，可是——

**达珀**：*（幕内。）*队长，我在这儿。

**费斯**：谁呀？——他来了，我想，博士。

**达珀**：说实话，实在对不住，队长。

**费斯**：我还满以为准能碰上你呢。

**达珀**：是啊，我真高兴。我得赶两份破诉状；昨晚又把怀表借给了一个今天上郡长家吃饭的人，结果连消遣的工夫也叫人劫走了。

**费斯**：这位就是他老大人。

**达珀**：他是一位博士？

**费斯**：是。

===Original===
**Dapper**：And have you broke with him, Captain?

**Face**：Ay.

**Dapper**：And how?

**Face**：Faith, he does make the matter, sir, so dainty I know not what to say.

**Dapper**：Not so, good Captain.

**Face**：Would I were fairly rid of it, believe me.

**Dapper**：Nay, now you grieve me, sir. Why should you wish so? I dare assure you, I'll not be ungrateful.

**Face**：I cannot think you will, sir. But the law Is such a thing—and then he says, Read's matter Falling so lately.

**Dapper**：Read! He was an ass, And dealt, sir, with a fool.

===Chinese===
**达珀**：您跟他提过了吗，队长？

**费斯**：提了。

**达珀**：结果如何？

**费斯**：说实话，先生，他把这事儿看得那么娇贵，我都不知道怎么说好。

**达珀**：不会的，好队长。

**费斯**：信我吧，真想干干净净脱了这个手。

**达珀**：哎，您这话叫我难受了，先生。您干吗盼着这样？我敢向您保证，我绝不是忘恩负义的人。

**费斯**：我也想您不会，先生。可法律那东西——他又提里德那桩案子，才了结没多久。

**达珀**：里德！他是头蠢驴，先生，他找的主儿是个傻子。

===Original===
**Face**：It was a clerk, sir.

**Dapper**：A clerk!

**Face**：Nay, hear me, sir. You know the law Better, I think—

**Dapper**：I should, sir, and the danger: You know, I showed the statute to you.

**Face**：You did so.

**Dapper**：And will I tell then! By this hand of flesh, Would it might never write good court-hand more, If I discover. What do you think of me, That I am a chiaus?

**Face**：What's that?

**Dapper**：The Turk was here. As one would say, do you think I am a Turk?

**Face**：I'll tell the Doctor so.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：那主儿是个文书，先生。

**达珀**：文书！

**费斯**：别急，听我说，先生。您懂法律，总比我懂——

**达珀**：我当然该懂，先生，还有那些凶险：您知道，法令条文还是我拿给您看的。

**费斯**：是你拿给我看的。

**达珀**：我会去告发才怪！凭我这只肉手，我要是泄露了半个字，叫它从此写不出一手好公文体。您把我当什么人了，当我是基亚乌斯（chiaus）吗？

**费斯**：那是什么？

**达珀**：土耳其人先前来过这儿。打个比方说：您当我是土耳其人吗？

**费斯**：我就这么回博士。

===Original===
**Dapper**：Do, good sweet Captain.

**Face**：Come, noble Doctor, pray thee let's prevail; This is the gentleman, and he is no chiaus.

**Subtle**：Captain, I have returned you all my answer. I would do much, sir, for your love—But this I neither may, nor can.

===Chinese===
**达珀**：劳驾了，好心的队长。

**费斯**：来吧，尊贵的博士，求你就成全了吧；这位是位体面绅士，可不是什么基亚乌斯。

**萨特尔**：队长，我的答复已经一句不剩都给你了。看在你的情分上，先生，我原可以效力良多——唯独这一桩，我不便，也不能。

===Original===
**Face**：Tut, do not say so. You deal now with a noble fellow, Doctor, One that will thank you richly; and he is no chiaus: Let that, sir, move you.

**Subtle**：Pray you, forbear—

**Face**：He has Four angels here.

**Subtle**：You do me wrong, good sir.

**Face**：Doctor, wherein? To tempt you with these spirits?

**Subtle**：To tempt my art and love, sir, to my peril. Fore heaven, I scarce can think you are my friend, That so would draw me to apparent danger.

**Face**：I draw you! A horse draw you, and a halter, You, and your flies together—

**Dapper**：Nay, good Captain.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：呸，别这么说。博士，你如今打交道的可是一位高贵的君子，会重重谢你的；他也不是什么基亚乌斯：就让这一点打动你吧，先生。

**萨特尔**：请你少来——

**费斯**：他这儿有四枚安琪尔金币（angel）。

**萨特尔**：您这是委屈我了，好先生。

**费斯**：博士，哪儿委屈了？拿这几位安琪尔「精灵」来诱惑您不成？

**萨特尔**：是拿我的艺术和交情往险地里诱，先生。皇天在上，我几乎要疑心您算不得我的朋友了，竟这样把我往明摆着的火坑里拽。

**费斯**：我拽您？让马拽了您去，外加一根绞索，您带着您那些苍蝇一块儿——

**达珀**：哎，好队长。

===Original===
**Face**：That know no difference of men.

**Subtle**：Good words, sir.

**Face**：Good deeds, sir, Doctor Dogs-meat. 'Slight, I bring you No cheating Clim o' the Cloughs or Claribels, That look as big as five-and-fifty, and flush; And spit out secrets like hot custard—

**Dapper**：Captain!

**Face**：Nor any melancholic under-scribe, Shall tell the vicar; but a special gentle, That is the heir to forty marks a year, Consorts with the small poets of the time, Is the sole hope of his old grandmother; That knows the law, and writes you six fair hands, Is a fine clerk, and has his ciphering perfect. Will take his oath o' the Greek Testament, If need be, in his pocket; and can court His mistress out of Ovid.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：连人的高低都分不清。

**萨特尔**：说话留点德，先生。

**费斯**：积点德行，先生，狗食博士。『上帝的』，我领来见您的，可不是冒牌的克利姆·奥·克拉夫（Clim o' the Clough），也不是克拉丽贝尔（Claribel）之流——那种人摆出五十五岁的威风，红光满面，吐起秘密来像吐热蛋奶冻——

**达珀**：队长！

**费斯**：也不是哪个愁眉苦脸的底下文书，转头就去向教区牧师告发；而是一位货真价实的上流公子：年进四十马克的继承人，同当今的小诗人们交游，是他老祖母独一份的指望；通晓法律，写得一手六体好字，是位体面文书，算术精熟；急起来能当场从口袋里掏出一册希腊文《新约》起誓；追求起情人来满口奥维德（Ovid）。

===Original===
**Dapper**：Nay, dear Captain—

**Face**：Did you not tell me so?

**Dapper**：Yes; but I'd have you Use master Doctor with some more respect.

**Face**：Hang him, proud stag, with his broad velvet head!— But for your sake, I'd choke, ere I would change An article of breath with such a puckfist: Come, let's be gone.

**Subtle**：Pray you let me speak with you.

**Dapper**：His worship calls you, Captain.

**Face**：I am sorry I e'er embarked myself in such a business.

===Chinese===
**达珀**：别这样，亲爱的队长——

**费斯**：这些话不是你自己跟我说的？

**达珀**：是；可我望您对博士先生多几分敬重。

**费斯**：去他的，神气的公鹿，顶着那颗宽天鹅绒脑袋！——不过看你的面子，我宁肯憋死，也不同这样一个放屁虫多换一口气：走，咱们走。

**萨特尔**：请留步，我跟你说句话。

**达珀**：他老大人叫你呢，队长。

**费斯**：我后悔不该沾手这桩营生。

===Original===
**Dapper**：Nay, good sir; he did call you.

**Face**：Will he take then?

**Subtle**：First, hear me—

**Face**：Not a syllable, 'less you take.

**Subtle**：Pray you, sir—

**Face**：Upon no terms but an *assumpsit*.

**Subtle**：Your humour must be law. *（He takes the four angels.）*

===Chinese===
**达珀**：哎，好先生；他确实叫你了。

**费斯**：那他肯收下了？

**萨特尔**：先听我说——

**费斯**：一个字也不听，除非你收下。

**萨特尔**：求您了，先生——

**费斯**：除非立下一纸 *assumpsit*（允诺契约），否则免谈。

**萨特尔**：你的癖性就是法律。*（他收下那四枚安琪尔金币。）*

===Original===
**Face**：Why now, sir, talk. Now I dare hear you with mine honour. Speak. So may this gentleman too.

**Subtle**：Why, sir— *（Offering to whisper **Face**.）*

**Face**：No whispering.

**Subtle**：Fore heaven, you do not apprehend the loss You do yourself in this.

**Face**：Wherein? For what?

**Subtle**：Marry, to be so importunate for one, That, when he has it, will undo you all: He'll win up all the money in the town.

**Face**：How!

**Subtle**：Yes, and blow up gamester after gamester, As they do crackers in a puppet-play. If I do give him a familiar, Give you him all you play for; never set him: For he will have it.

**Face**：You are mistaken, Doctor. Why he does ask one but for cups and horses, A rifling fly; none of your great familiars.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：这才对，先生，说吧。现在我敢拿名誉听你讲了。说吧。这位先生也可以听。

**萨特尔**：是这样，先生—— *（作势要与**费斯**咬耳朵。）*

**费斯**：不许咬耳朵。

**萨特尔**：皇天在上，您还没明白，您这样要自误多少利钱。

**费斯**：自误在哪儿？为什么？

**萨特尔**：唉呀，为了这么一个人死缠硬磨；等他到手了，要把你们通通毁了：他会把全城的钱赢个干干净净。

**费斯**：什么！

**萨特尔**：是，还要把赌客一个接一个轰上天，像木偶戏里放炮仗。我要真给他一个侍灵，你们往后同他赌，就只管把赌注全数奉送；千万别同他对赌：因为钱准到他手里。

**费斯**：你弄错了，博士。他求侍灵不过为着赢杯盏、赌马，一只捞钱的飞虫罢了；不是你们那号了不起的大侍灵。

===Original===
**Dapper**：Yes, Captain, I would have it for all games.

**Subtle**：I told you so.

**Face**：*（Taking **Dapper** aside.）* 'Slight, that is a new business! I understood you, a tame bird, to fly Twice in a term, or so, on Friday nights, When you had left the office, for a nag Of forty or fifty shillings.

**Dapper**：Ay, 'tis true, sir; But I do think now I shall leave the law, And therefore—

**Face**：Why, this changes quite the case. Do you think that I dare move him?

**Dapper**：If you please, sir; All's one to him, I see.

**Face**：What! For that money? I cannot with my conscience; nor should you Make the request, methinks.

**Dapper**：No, sir, I mean To add consideration.

**Face**：Why then, sir, I'll try.— *（Goes to **Subtle**.）* Say that it were for all games, Doctor.

===Chinese===
**达珀**：要的，队长，我各样赌戏都想用它。

**萨特尔**：我说什么来着。

**费斯**：*（把**达珀**拉到一旁。）*『上帝的』，这可是另一桩新买卖！我原当你不过养只驯鸟，一个开庭季里，顶多星期五晚上下班之后，为着一匹四五十先令的小马飞上个一两回。

**达珀**：是，是这样，先生；可我如今想要抛下法律这行，所以——

**费斯**：哦，那可全然是另一回事了。你想我还敢向他开口吗？

**达珀**：您肯的话就开这个口，先生；我看他说什么都成。

**费斯**：什么！就为那几个钱？我良心上说不过去；我想，连您也不该开这个口。

**达珀**：不是，先生，我是说要加谢仪。

**费斯**：那样的话，先生，我就试试。——*（走向**萨特尔**。）*假如是为着各样赌戏呢，博士。

===Original===
**Subtle**：I say then, not a mouth shall eat for him At any ordinary, but on the score, That is a gaming mouth, conceive me.

**Face**：Indeed!

**Subtle**：He'll draw you all the treasure of the realm, If it be set him.

**Face**：Speak you this from art?

**Subtle**：Ay, sir, and reason too, the ground of art. He is of the only best complexion, The Queen of Fairy loves.

**Face**：What! Is he?

**Subtle**：Peace. He'll overhear you. Sir, should she but see him—

**Face**：What?

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：那我就说：往后随便哪家包饭馆，谁也别想同他同桌吃饭，除非挂账——我说的是张赌嘴，您明白我的意思。

**费斯**：竟这样！

**萨特尔**：只要有人同他对赌，他能把全国的财宝都吸到自己身边来。

**费斯**：这话是从法术里看出来的？

**萨特尔**：是，先生，还有道理——法术的地基。他生就那独一无二的最好面色，正是仙后偏爱的。

**费斯**：什么！他真这样？

**萨特尔**：嘘。他会听见的。先生，她老人家只要见他一面——

**费斯**：怎样？

===Original===
**Subtle**：Do not you tell him.

**Face**：Will he win at cards too?

**Subtle**：The spirits of dead Holland, living Isaac, You'd swear, were in him; such a vigorous luck As cannot be resisted. 'Slight, he'll put Six of your gallants to a cloak, indeed.

**Face**：A strange success, that some man shall be born to.

**Subtle**：He hears you, man—

**Dapper**：Sir, I'll not be ingrateful.

**Face**：Faith, I have confidence in his good nature: You hear, he says he will not be ingrateful.

**Subtle**：Why, as you please; my venture follows yours.

**Face**：Troth, do it, Doctor; think him trusty, and make him. He may make us both happy in an hour; Win some five thousand pound, and send us two on't.

**Dapper**：Believe it, and I will, sir.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：你可别告诉他。

**费斯**：他赌牌也会赢？

**萨特尔**：死「荷兰」、活「艾萨克」的赌魂——都是眼下的赌戏名——您会赌咒说全附在他身上；那样一股挡不住的旺运。『上帝的』，他真能叫六位纨绔合赔他一件斗篷，不骗你。

**费斯**：真是奇造化，偏偏叫某个人生下来承受。

**萨特尔**：他在听呢，老兄——

**达珀**：先生，我不会忘恩负义的。

**费斯**：说实话，我也信他的好心肠：你听见了，他说他不会忘恩负义。

**萨特尔**：唉，随你们吧；我的股子跟着你们的走。

**费斯**：真的，做吧，博士；就把他当个靠得住的人，也把他造成个靠得住的人。他兴许一个钟头里就让你我皆大欢喜：赢上五千镑上下，分咱们两千。

**达珀**：放心，我一定办到，先生。

===Original===
**Face**：And you shall, sir. *（Takes him aside.）* You have heard all?

**Dapper**：No, what was't? Nothing, I, sir.

**Face**：Nothing!

**Dapper**：A little, sir.

**Face**：Well, a rare star Reigned at your birth.

**Dapper**：At mine, sir! No.

**Face**：The Doctor Swears that you are—

**Subtle**：Nay, Captain, you'll tell all now.

**Face**：Allied to the Queen of Fairy.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：你自然办得到，先生。*（把他拉到一旁。）*你都听见了？

**达珀**：没有，说什么了？什么也没……有，先生。

**费斯**：什么也没！

**达珀**：有一点点，先生。

**费斯**：好啊，你降生的时候，天上正照着一颗难得的吉星。

**达珀**：我头上，先生！哪有的事。

**费斯**：博士赌咒说，你是——

**萨特尔**：得了，队长，你这就全抖出来了。

**费斯**：同仙后有亲。

===Original===
**Dapper**：Who! That I am? Believe it, no such matter—

**Face**：Yes, and that You were born with a cawl on your head.

**Dapper**：Who says so?

**Face**：Come, You know it well enough, though you dissemble it.

**Dapper**：I'fac, I do not; you are mistaken.

**Face**：How! Swear by your fac, and in a thing so known Unto the Doctor? How shall we, sir, trust you In the other matter? Can we ever think, When you have won five or six thousand pound, You'll send us shares in't, by this rate?

**Dapper**：By Jove, sir, I'll win ten thousand pound, and send you half. I'fac's no oath.

**Subtle**：No, no, he did but jest.

===Chinese===
**达珀**：谁！我是？您可别信，没影儿的事——

**费斯**：是有这事，还说你生下来头上就罩着一层胎膜。

**达珀**：谁说的？

**费斯**：得了，你自己清楚得很，何必装蒜。

**达珀**：凭这张脸起誓，我真不知道；您弄错了。

**费斯**：什么！凭你的「脸」起誓，还是在博士一清二楚的事上？先生，这样我们哪敢把另一桩事托付给你？照这个样子，等你赢了五六千镑，还能指望你分我们股份？

**达珀**：凭朱庇特起誓，先生，我要赢一万镑，分你们一半。「凭脸」不算誓言。

**萨特尔**：不，不，他不过说笑。

===Original===
**Face**：Go to. Go thank the Doctor: he's your friend, To take it so.

**Dapper**：I thank his worship.

**Face**：So! Another angel.

**Dapper**：Must I?

**Face**：Must you! 'Slight, What else is thanks? Will you be trivial?—Doctor, *（**Dapper** gives him the money.）* When must he come for his familiar?

**Dapper**：Shall I not have it with me?

**Subtle**：O, good sir! There must a world of ceremonies pass; You must be bathed and fumigated first: Besides the Queen of Fairy does not rise Till it be noon.

**Face**：Not, if she danced, tonight.

**Subtle**：And she must bless it.

**Face**：Did you never see Her royal Grace yet?

**Dapper**：Whom?

**Face**：Your aunt of Fairy?

**Subtle**：Not since she kissed him in the cradle, Captain; I can resolve you that.

**Face**：Well, see her Grace, Whate'er it cost you, for a thing that I know. It will be somewhat hard to compass; but However, see her. You are made, believe it, If you can see her. Her Grace is a lone woman, And very rich; and if she take a fancy, She will do strange things. See her, at any hand. 'Slid, she may hap to leave you all she has: It is the Doctor's fear.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：去去。去谢博士：他肯这么担待，是你的朋友。

**达珀**：谢他老人家。

**费斯**：这就对喽！再来一枚安琪尔金币。

**达珀**：非给不可？

**费斯**：那还用问！『上帝的』，不然还怎么叫道谢？你就这么小家子气？——博士，*（**达珀**把钱交给他。）*他该几时来领他的侍灵？

**达珀**：不能当场带走吗？

**萨特尔**：哎，好先生！还得过上一大套仪程呢；您得先行沐浴、受熏香：再说，仙后不到正午是不起身的。

**费斯**：除非她今夜跳了舞。

**萨特尔**：她还须亲自为它祝圣。

**费斯**：你还从没见过她老人家圣颜吧？

**达珀**：谁？

**费斯**：你那位仙界姑母。

**萨特尔**：打她在摇篮里亲过他那一回起就没见过，队长；这话我可以给你个准信。

**费斯**：那么，去见她老人家；不管破费多少，为着一桩我心里有数的事。这事情要办成有点难；可是无论如何，去见她。只要你见得着她，你就发定了，信我的。她老人家是个独身妇人，又有的是钱；她若是一时看中了谁，什么奇事都做得出来。无论如何要去见她。『圣母的』，她兴许一时高兴，把全部家当都留给你：这正是博士担着的心事。

===Original===
**Dapper**：How will't be done, then?

**Face**：Let me alone, take you no thought. Do you But say to me, Captain, I'll see her Grace.

**Dapper**：“Captain, I'll see her Grace.”

**Face**：Enough.

**Subtle**：Who's there? Anon. *（Aside to **Face**.）* —Conduct him forth by the back way.— Sir, against one o'clock prepare yourself; Till when you must be fasting; only take Three drops of vinegar in at your nose, Two at your mouth, and one at either ear; Then bathe your fingers' ends and wash your eyes, To sharpen your five senses, and cry “hum” Thrice, and then “buz” as often; and then come.

**Face**：Can you remember this?

**Dapper**：I warrant you.

**Face**：Well then, away. It is but your bestowing Some twenty nobles 'mong her Grace's servants, And put on a clean shirt: you do not know What grace her Grace may do you in clean linen.

===Chinese===
**达珀**：那可怎么办呢？

**费斯**：交给我，你什么心也不用操。你只消对我说一句：队长，我要见她老人家。

**达珀**：「队长，我要见她老人家。」

**费斯**：足够了。

**萨特尔**：谁呀？来了。*（向**费斯**旁白。）*——从后门把他送出去。——先生，一点钟整，请您准备停当；在那之前须得斋戒；只消往鼻子里点三滴醋，嘴里两滴，两耳各一滴；再把指尖浸一浸、眼睛洗一洗，好教五官敏锐，然后喊三声「嗡」，再照样喊那么多声「巴兹」；然后就过来。

**费斯**：这些你记得住？

**达珀**：包在我身上。

**费斯**：那么走吧。你只消在她老人家仆从们身上赏个二十来枚诺布尔金币（noble），再换上一件干净衬衫：你可不知道，一个穿干净亚麻衫的人，能从「恩典」老太太手里讨来多大的恩典。

===Original===
**Subtle**：*（Within.）* Come in! Good wives, I pray you forbear me now; Troth I can do you no good till afternoon—

**Drugger**：Yes, sir.

**Subtle**：A seller of tobacco?

**Drugger**：Yes, sir.

**Subtle**：Umph! Free of the grocers?

**Drugger**：Ay, and't please you.

**Subtle**：Well— Your business, Abel?

**Drugger**：This, and't please your worship; I am a young beginner, and am building Of a new shop, and't like your worship, just At corner of a street:—Here is the plot on't— And I would know by art, sir, of your worship, Which way I should make my door, by necromancy, And where my shelves; and which should be for boxes, And which for pots. I would be glad to thrive, sir: And I was wished to your worship by a gentleman, One Captain Face, that says you know men's planets, And their good angels, and their bad.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：*（幕内。）*进来吧！好太太们，求你们这会儿先饶了我；说实话，不到下午，我实在帮不上你们的忙——

**德拉格**：是，先生。

**萨特尔**：卖烟草的？

**德拉格**：是，先生。

**萨特尔**：嗯！杂货行会（grocers）的在册会员？

**德拉格**：是，如您所问。

**萨特尔**：好——你来求什么，亚伯？

**德拉格**：是这样，您老大人若肯垂听：我是个初出茅庐的后生，正在起一座新铺面，若蒙您老大人俯允，恰好在一条街的拐角上：——图样在这儿——我想借您老大人的法术请教：门该朝哪边开——用死灵术（necromancy）定夺——货架该设在哪里；哪几格放盒子，哪几格放坛子。我是诚心巴望兴旺，先生：有位绅士指点我来求您老大人，是费斯队长，说您通晓世人的行星，认得他们的善星和凶星。

===Original===
**Subtle**：I do, If I do see them—

**Face**：What! My honest Abel? Though art well met here.

**Drugger**：Troth, sir, I was speaking, Just as your worship came here, of your worship: I pray you speak for me to Master Doctor.

**Face**：He shall do anything.—Doctor, do you hear? This is my friend, Abel, an honest fellow; He lets me have good tobacco, and he does not Sophisticate it with sack-lees or oil, Nor washes it in muscadel and grains, Nor buries it in gravel, under ground, Wrapped up in greasy leather, or pissed clouts: But keeps it in fine lily pots, that, opened, Smell like conserve of roses, or French beans. He has his maple block, his silver tongs, Winchester pipes, and fire of Juniper: A neat, spruce, honest fellow, and no goldsmith.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：通晓，只要他们肯到我跟前来——

**费斯**：哎哟！我老实的亚伯？在这儿遇上你，再巧不过。

**德拉格**：真的，先生，您老大人进来的当口，我正说到您老大人呢：求您替我在博士先生跟前美言几句。

**费斯**：他什么事都好商量。——博士，你听着？这是我的朋友亚伯，个老实人；他给我的烟向来地道，从不用白酒渣或油类掺假，不拿麝香白酒和染料汤洗烟叶，也不把它裹在油腻皮革或尿布里、埋进地下的沙砾堆，而是收在精细的百合花坛罐里，一揭盖，香气像玫瑰酱，又像法国豆。他有枫木烟砧，银烟钳，温彻斯特（Winchester）烟斗，烧的是杜松枝的火：一个爽利齐整、诚实的汉子，不是金匠那路人。

===Original===
**Subtle**：He is a fortunate fellow, that I am sure on.

**Face**：Already, sir, have you found it? Lo thee, Abel!

**Subtle**：And in right way toward riches—

**Face**：Sir!

**Subtle**：This summer He will be of the clothing of his company, And next spring called to the scarlet; spend what he can.

**Face**：What, and so little beard?

**Subtle**：Sir, you must think, He may have a receipt to make hair come: But he'll be wise, preserve his youth, and fine for't; His fortune looks for him another way.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：他是个有福的汉子，这个我拿得准。

**费斯**：这就掂量出来了，先生？你听见没有，亚伯！

**萨特尔**：而且正走在发财的正道上——

**费斯**：哦？

**萨特尔**：今年夏天，他就会领上他行会的袍服；来年开春，就要被召穿猩红袍了；随他怎么破费也拦不住。

**费斯**：什么，胡子就这么几根？

**萨特尔**：先生，您要想得到：想生须发，他尽有方子；可他精明着呢，宁可保住少年面相，认罚了事；他的福运在另一条道上候着他。

===Original===
**Face**：'Slid, Doctor, how canst thou know this so soon? I am amazed at that!

**Subtle**：By a rule, Captain, In metoposcopy, which I do work by; A certain star in the forehead, which you see not. Your chestnut or your olive-coloured face Does never fail: and your long ear doth promise. I knew't by certain spots, too, in his teeth, And on the nail of his mercurial finger.

**Face**：Which finger's that?

**Subtle**：His little finger. Look. You were born upon a Wednesday?

**Drugger**：Yes, indeed, sir.

**Subtle**：The thumb, in chiromancy, we give Venus; The forefinger, to Jove; the midst, to Saturn; The ring, to Sol; the least, to Mercury, Who was the lord, sir, of his horoscope, His house of life being Libra; which foreshowed, He should be a merchant, and should trade with balance.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：『圣母的』，博士，你怎么这样快就算出来了？我真给这事惊呆了！

**萨特尔**：凭一条法则，队长，我行的是额相术（metoposcopy）：额头上有一颗星，您瞧不见。栗子色也好，橄榄色也好，这种脸从不应错：他这双长耳朵也是福相。我还凭他牙齿上的几点斑、他水星指指甲上的记号认出来的。

**费斯**：哪根是水星指？

**萨特尔**：他那根小指。您瞧。您是礼拜三生的？

**德拉格**：正是，先生。

**萨特尔**：手相术（chiromancy）里，拇指归维纳斯；食指归朱庇特；中指归萨图恩；无名指归太阳；小指归水星——而水星，先生，正是他星命盘的主星，命宫落在天秤宫（Libra）：这预示他当作个商人，凭天平做买卖。

===Original===
**Face**：Why, this is strange! Is it not, honest Nab?

**Subtle**：There is a ship now, coming from Ormus, That shall yield him such a commodity Of drugs *（Pointing to the plan.）* —This is the west, and this the south?

**Drugger**：Yes, sir.

**Subtle**：And those are your two sides?

**Drugger**：Ay, sir.

**Subtle**：Make me your door, then, south; your broad side, west: And on the east side of your shop, aloft, Write Mathlai, Tarmiel, and Baraborat; Upon the north part, Rael, Velel, Thiel. They are the names of those mercurial spirits, That do fright flies from boxes.

**Drugger**：Yes, sir.

**Subtle**：And Beneath your threshold, bury me a loadstone To draw in gallants that wear spurs: the rest, They'll seem to follow.

**Face**：That's a secret, Nab!

**Subtle**：And, on your stall, a puppet, with a vice And a court-fucus to call city-dames: You shall deal much with minerals.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：咦，这可神了！是不是，老实的纳布？

**萨特尔**：眼下正有一条船从霍尔木兹（Ormus）来，会给他带来那么一批药材好买卖。*（指着图样。）*——这边是西，这边是南？

**德拉格**：是，先生。

**萨特尔**：那两边是您的铺面？

**德拉格**：是，先生。

**萨特尔**：那就把门给我开在南边；宽面朝西：铺子东面高处，写上 Mathlai、Tarmiel、Baraborat；北面写 Rael、Velel、Thiel。这是那些水星精灵的名字，专管把苍蝇从盒子上吓退。

**德拉格**：是，先生。

**萨特尔**：还有，在门槛底下给我埋一块磁石，好把戴马刺的纨绔们吸进门来：其余的客人，自会跟着来的样子。

**费斯**：这可是秘诀，纳布！

**萨特尔**：还有，柜台上摆一个木偶，带一个「恶童」搭档（vice，木偶戏丑角），再配一味宫廷脂粉（court-fucus），好招引城里的太太们：您往后要同矿物打不少交道。

===Original===
**Drugger**：Sir, I have. At home, already—

**Subtle**：Ay, I know you have arsenic, Vitriol, sal-tartar, argaile, alkali, Cinoper: I know all.—This fellow, Captain, Will come, in time, to be a great distiller, And give a say—I will not say directly, But very fair—at the philosopher's stone.

**Face**：Why, how now, Abel! Is this true?

**Drugger**：*（Aside to **Face**.）* Good Captain, What must I give?

**Face**：Nay, I'll not counsel thee. Thou hear'st what wealth (he says, spend what thou canst,) Thou'rt like to come to.

**Drugger**：I would gi' him a crown.

**Face**：A crown! And toward such a fortune? Heart, Thou shalt rather gi' him thy shop. No gold about thee?

**Drugger**：Yes, I have a portague, I have kept this half-year.

**Face**：Out on thee, Nab! 'Slight, there was such an offer— Shalt keep't no longer, I'll give't him for thee. Doctor, Nab prays your worship to drink this, and swears He will appear more grateful, as your skill Does raise him in the world.

===Chinese===
**德拉格**：先生，我已经有了。家里现成——

**萨特尔**：是，我知道你有砒霜、绿矾、酒石盐（sal-tartar）、粗酒石（argaile）、碱、朱砂（cinoper）：我全知道。——这个汉子，队长，假以时日，会成为一位了不起的蒸馏名家，稳稳问上一句——我不说一箭正中，至少八九不离十——哲人石。

**费斯**：咦，怎么样，亚伯！这话当真？

**德拉格**：*（向**费斯**旁白。）*好队长，我该奉上多少？

**费斯**：不，我可不能替你出主意。你听见他说的那份家业了（他说，随你怎么花）——眼看就要落到你头上了。

**德拉格**：我想奉上一枚克朗。

**费斯**：一枚克朗！对着这样一份福运？呸，你还不如把铺子白送他。身上没带金子？

**德拉格**：带了，我有一枚葡萄牙金币（portague），攒了半年了。

**费斯**：真没出息，纳布！『上帝的』，这可是打着灯笼难找的机会——这钱你别想再留了，我替你送给他。博士，纳布求您老大人喝了这杯，还发誓说，随着您的法术把他往世上高处提携，他往后必当加倍报答。

===Original===
**Drugger**：I would entreat Another favour of his worship.

**Face**：What is't, Nab?

**Drugger**：But to look over, sir, my almanac, And cross out my ill-days, that I may neither Bargain, nor trust upon them.

**Face**：That he shall, Nab: Leave it, it shall be done, 'gainst afternoon.

**Subtle**：And a direction for his shelves.

**Face**：Now, Nab, Art thou well pleased, Nab?

**Drugger**：'Thank, sir, both your worships.

**Face**：Away.

**Subtle**：You are pleasant, sir.

===Chinese===
**德拉格**：我还想求他老人家另一件事。

**费斯**：什么事，纳布？

**德拉格**：不过求他老人家把我的历书过目一遍，把凶日都划掉，好叫我在那些日子既不订买卖，也不放赊账。

**费斯**：这个自然应允，纳布：留下吧，下午以前给你办妥。

**萨特尔**：再给他的货架批一个吉向。

**费斯**：怎么样，纳布，你可称心如意了，纳布？

**德拉格**：多谢，先生，谢两位老大人。

**费斯**：去吧。

**萨特尔**：您可真爱说笑，先生。

===Original===
**Dol Common**：Yonder fishwife Will not away. And there's your giantess, The bawd of Lambeth.

**Subtle**：Heart, I cannot speak with them.

**Dol Common**：Not afore night, I have told them in a voice, Thorough the trunk, like one of your familiars. But I have spied sir Epicure Mammon—

**Subtle**：Where?

**Dol Common**：Coming along, at far end of the lane, Slow of his feet, but earnest of his tongue To one that's with him.

**Subtle**：Face, go you and shift.

**Dol Common**：Why, what's the matter?

**Subtle**：O, I did look for him With the sun's rising: 'marvel he could sleep, This is the day I am to perfect for him The *magisterium*, our great work, the stone; And yield it, made, into his hands: of which He has, this month, talked as he were possessed. And now he's dealing pieces on't away.— Methinks I see him entering ordinaries, Dispensing for the pox, and plaguey houses, Reaching his dose, walking Moorfields for lepers, And offering citizens' wives pomander-bracelets, As his preservative, made of the elixir; Searching the spittal, to make old bawds young; And the highways, for beggars, to make rich. I see no end of his labours. He will make Nature ashamed of her long sleep: when art, Who's but a step-dame, shall do more than she, In her best love to mankind, ever could: If his dream lasts, he'll turn the age to gold.

===Chinese===
**多尔·康门**：那边的鱼贩婆赖着不肯走。还有你那位女巨人，兰贝斯（Lambeth）来的鸨母。

**萨特尔**：呸，我见不了她们。

**多尔·康门**：天黑前都不成，我已拿腔拿调隔着传话筒打发过她们了，学的是你那些侍灵的嗓音。可我望见了伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士——

**萨特尔**：在哪儿？

**多尔·康门**：正顺着巷子那头走来，脚下慢吞吞，舌头倒卖力，一路跟身旁那位说个不住。

**萨特尔**：费斯，你去换行头。

**多尔·康门**：咦，什么事这样急？

**萨特尔**：哦，我原算定他该随日出就到：难怪他竟睡得着。今天正是我该替他把 *magisterium*（大匠秘药）——咱们的大功、哲人石——炼至圆满的日子，要把炼成的成品交到他手里：为着它，他这一个月说起话来都像着了魔。此刻他只怕正一撮一撮把它散给旁人呢。——我仿佛看见他闯进一家家包饭馆，为梅毒和瘟疫人家施药，挨门递上他的药剂；踱进穆尔原野（Moorfields）寻访麻风病人；向市民太太们献香丸手镯，说是拿灵药（elixir）制的避疫圣品；巡到麻风院，教老鸨们返老还童；巡到官道，教叫花子个个变富。他的功德看不到边际。他要叫大自然为她这一场长眠羞愧：艺术不过是个继母，却要做出她以对人类最深的慈爱从不曾做到的事。他这场美梦只要不醒，他要把整个时代点化成金。

## Act II / 第二幕

### Scene I / 第二场

===Original===
An outer room in **Lovewit’s** house.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Come on, sir. Now, you set your foot on shore In *Novo Orbe*; here’s the rich Peru: And there within, sir, are the golden mines, Great Solomon’s Ophir! He was sailing to’t, Three years, but we have reached it in ten months. This is the day, wherein, to all my friends, I will pronounce the happy word, *Be Rich*; *This day you shall be *spectatissimi**. You shall no more deal with the hollow dye, Or the frail card. No more be at charge of keeping The livery-punk for the young heir, that must Seal, at all hours, in his shirt: no more, If he deny, have him beaten to’t, as he is That brings him the commodity. No more Shall thirst of satin, or the covetous hunger Of velvet entrails for a rude-spun cloak, To be displayed at Madam Augusta’s, make The sons of Sword and Hazard fall before The golden calf, and on their knees, whole nights Commit idolatry with wine and trumpets: Or go a feasting after drum and ensign. No more of this. You shall start up young viceroys, And have your punks, and punketees, my Surly. And unto thee I speak it first, *Be Rich*. Where is my Subtle, there? Within, ho!

**Face**：*（Within.）* Sir, He’ll come to you by and by.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：That is his firedrake, His Lungs, his Zephyrus, he that puffs his coals, Till he firk nature up, in her own centre. You are not faithful, sir. This night, I’ll change All that is metal, in my house, to gold: And, early in the morning, will I send To all the plumbers and the pewterers, And by their tin and lead up; and to Lothbury For all the copper.

**Pertinax Surly**：What, and turn that too?

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Yes, and I’ll purchase Devonshire and Cornwall, And make them perfect Indies! You admire now?

===Chinese===
**洛维特**宅中的一间外屋。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：来吧，先生。此刻，您一脚踏上了*Novo Orbe*（新大陆）的岸；这儿就是富饶的秘鲁：那边里头，先生，就是金矿，伟大的所罗门的俄斐（Ophir）！他扬帆驶了三年才到，咱们十个月就摸着了。就是今天，我要向所有朋友宣布那句吉祥话：*Be Rich*（发财吧）；今天你们就要成为 *spectatissimi*（最显贵的人）。你们再不用去对付灌了铅的骰子，或是那副靠不住的纸牌。再不用破费养着一个穿号衣的暗娼，去伺候那位动不动就穿着衬衫盖章的年轻嗣子；也再不用在他赖账时，教人把送「货」来的那一位打一顿，逼他就范。再不会让缎子的渴、天鹅绒脏腑对一件粗纺斗篷的贪馋——只为了到奥古斯塔夫人那儿亮相——逼得「刀剑与赌命」之子们拜倒在金犊面前，跪上整夜整夜，就着酒和号角行偶像崇拜；或者跟着鼓声军旗去赶酒宴。再没有这些了。你们一个个要腾地而起，做年轻的总督，拥上你们的娘子和小小娘，我的瑟利。这话我先对你说：*Be Rich*（发财吧）。我的萨特尔呢，在吗？里头，喂！

**费斯**：*（幕内。）*先生，他这就来见您。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：那是他的火龙，他的「肺哥」，他的西风神（Zephyrus），替他吹炭火的人，直把「自然」在她自家的炉心里捅得旺起来。您不够虔诚哪，先生。就在今夜，我要把宅子里一切金属统统变成金子；明儿一早，我就派人去见所有的铅管匠和锡器匠，把他们的锡和铅统统买断；再上洛斯伯里（Lothbury，铜匠街）把铜全数包圆。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：什么，连那些也要点成金子？

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：是，我还要买下德文郡和康沃尔郡，把它们点成地地道道的印度！这会儿您惊呆了？

===Original===
**Pertinax Surly**：No, faith.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：But when you see th’ effects of the Great Medicine, Of which one part projected on a hundred Of Mercury, or Venus, or the moon, Shall turn it to as many of the sun; Nay, to a thousand, so *ad infinitum*: You will believe me.

**Pertinax Surly**：Yes, when I see’t, I will. But if my eyes do cozen me so, and I Giving them no occasion, sure I’ll have A whore, shall piss them out next day.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Ha! Why? Do you think I fable with you? I assure you, He that has once the flower of the sun, The perfect ruby, which we call elixir, Not only can do that, but, by its virtue, Can confer honour, love, respect, long life; Give safety, valour, yea, and victory, To whom he will. In eight and twenty days, I’ll make an old man of fourscore, a child.

**Pertinax Surly**：No doubt; he’s that already.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Nay, I mean, Restore his years, renew him, like an eagle, To the fifth age; make him get sons and daughters, Young giants; as our philosophers have done, The ancient patriarchs, afore the flood, But taking, once a week, on a knife’s point, The quantity of a grain of mustard of it; Become stout Marses, and beget young Cupids.

**Pertinax Surly**：The decayed Vestals of Pict-hatch would thank you, That keep the fire alive, there.

===Chinese===
**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：没有，实话。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：可等您亲眼见了「大药」（Great Medicine）的功效——一份「投射」到一百份水星（汞）、金星（铜）或月亮（银）上，就能把它们尽数点成同等数量的太阳（金）；不，点成一千份，如此 *ad infinitum*（以至无穷）——那时您就信我了。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：好，等我亲眼见了，我就信。可要是我的眼睛这样骗我，而我又没给它们半点由头，我一定找个婊子，第二天叫她把这对眼珠子尿出来。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：哈！怎么？您当我同您编故事？我向您担保，谁一旦得了「太阳之花」，那完美的红宝石——我们唤作灵药的——不仅能办到这些，还凭它的德性，能赐人荣名、爱情、敬重、长寿；赐人平安、勇武，是了，还有胜利，随他高兴给谁。二十八天之内，我能把一个八十岁的老头变回孩童。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：那还用说；他现在就已经是了。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：不，我是说，还他年岁，让他焕然一新，像鹰一样重返第五纪；叫他生养儿女，一帮小巨人；正如我们的哲人们做过的那样——洪荒之前的古圣先祖，每礼拜一次，在刀尖上取一粒芥子那么多，便都成了雄赳赳的玛尔斯，生养出一窝窝小丘比特。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：皮克特庄（Pict-hatch）那班歇了业的维斯塔贞女倒要谢谢您，她们那儿正靠人把火种传得不灭呢。

===Original===
**Sir Epicure Mammon**：’Tis the secret Of nature naturised ’gainst all infections, Cures all diseases coming of all causes; A month’s grief in a day, a year’s in twelve; And, of what age soever, in a month: Past all the doses of your drugging doctors. I’ll undertake, withal, to fright the plague Out of the kingdom in three months.

**Pertinax Surly**：And I’ll Be bound, the players shall sing your praises, then, Without their poets.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Sir, I’ll do’t. Meantime, I’ll give away so much unto my man, Shall serve the whole city, with preservative Weekly; each house his dose, and at the rate—

**Pertinax Surly**：As he that built the waterwork, does with water?

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：You are incredulous.

**Pertinax Surly**：Faith I have a humour, I would not willingly be gulled. Your stone Cannot transmute me.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Pertinax, [my] Surly, Will you believe antiquity? Records? I’ll show you a book where Moses and his sister, And Solomon have written of the art; Ay, and a treatise penned by Adam—

===Chinese===
**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：这是「自然化成的自然」的秘密，抵御一切瘟毒；包治百病，不问病因：一月的沉疴一天去，一年的十二天去；任凭多大年纪，一月之内去根：甩开你们那班灌药的郎中一切剂量。我还立一张包票：三个月内，把瘟疫吓得逃出王国。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：那我也立一票：到那天戏子们不用诗人们代笔，自会唱您的赞歌。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：先生，我办得到。这期间，我要拨出那么多药给我的仆人，叫他每周给全城施送预防剂；每家一份，价钱嘛——

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：跟那位修水厂的卖水一个价？

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：您真是没信心。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：说实话我有种癖性，不愿轻易上当。您那石头，嬗变不了我。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：珀蒂纳克斯，我的瑟利，您信不信古董？信不信记载？我给您看一部书，摩西和他姐姐、还有所罗门，都亲笔写过这门艺术；对，还有一部亚当亲笔的论著——

===Original===
**Pertinax Surly**：How!

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Of the philosopher’s stone, and in High Dutch.

**Pertinax Surly**：Did Adam write, sir, in High Dutch?

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：He did; Which proves it was the primitive tongue.

**Pertinax Surly**：What paper?

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：On cedar board.

**Pertinax Surly**：O that, indeed, they say, Will last ’gainst worms.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：’Tis like your Irish wood, ’Gainst cobwebs. I have a piece of Jason’s fleece, too, Which was no other than a book of alchemy, Writ in large sheepskin, a good fat ram-vellum. Such was Pythagoras’ thigh, Pandora’s tub, And, all that fable of Medea’s charms, The manner of our work; the bulls, our furnace, Still breathing fire; our argent-vive, the dragon: The dragon’s teeth, mercury sublimate, That keeps the whiteness, hardness, and the biting; And they are gathered into Jason’s helm, The alembic, and then sowed in Mars his field, And thence sublimed so often, till they’re fixed. Both this, the Hesperian garden, Cadmus’ story, Jove’s shower, the boon of Midas, Argus’ eyes, Boccace his Demogorgon, thousands more, All abstract riddles of our stone.

===Chinese===
**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：什么！

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：论哲人石的，用高地德语写的。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：亚当写字用高地德语吗，先生？

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：用的；这正好证明德语是原始的语言。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：写在什么纸上？

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：雪松木板上。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：哦，那个嘛，的确，人家都说，防得住蠹虫。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：就跟您那爱尔兰木料防蜘蛛网一样。我还有一片伊阿宋的金羊毛，那原不过是一部炼金书，写在大张羊皮上，上好的肥公羊羔皮。毕达哥拉斯的金大腿、潘多拉的瓮，还有美狄亚魔咒的整套传说，也都是这样——全是我们工法的高言：那两头喷火的神牛，是我们的炉子，不住地吐火；我们的水银（argent-vive）就是那头龙；龙牙，是升华汞，保着那白度、硬度与咬劲；它们收进伊阿宋的头盔——也就是蒸馏盔（alembic）——再播种到马尔斯的田里，从此一而再、再而三地升华，直到固定。还有这金羊毛、赫斯珀里得斯的园子、卡德摩斯的故事、朱庇特的金雨、弥达斯的恩赐、阿耳戈斯的眼睛、薄伽丘（Boccace）笔下的得墨戈耳工（Demogorgon），成千上万——全是咱们石头的玄谜隐语。

===Original===
**Face**：The evening will set red upon you, sir; You have colour for it, crimson: the red ferment Has done his office; three hours hence prepare you To see projection.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Pertinax, my Surly. Again I say to thee, aloud, *Be rich*. This day, thou shalt have ingots; and tomorrow, Give lords th’ affront.—Is it, my Zephyrus, right? Blushes the bolt’s head?

**Face**：Like a wench with child, sir, That were but now discovered to her master.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Excellent witty Lungs!—my only care Where to get stuff enough now, to project on; This town will not half serve me.

**Face**：No, sir! Buy The covering off o’ churches.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：That’s true.

**Face**：Yes. Let them stand bare, as do their auditory; Or cap them, new, with shingles.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：No, good thatch: Thatch will lie light upon the rafters, Lungs.— Lungs, I will manumit thee from the furnace; I will restore thee thy complexion, Puffe, Lost in the embers; and repair this brain, Hurt with the fume o’ the metals.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：晚祷的时辰就要给您披上红色了，先生；您也有这个气色，绯红：红色发酵已经当完了它的差；三个钟头后，请您预备观看投射（点金）。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：珀蒂纳克斯，我的瑟利。我再大声对你说一遍：*Be rich*（发财吧）。今天，你就要捧上金锭；明天，只管打公卿们的脸。——怎么样，我的西风神？对不对？长颈瓶红了脸没有？

**费斯**：红得像个刚被主人发觉有了身孕的丫头，先生。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：妙人妙语的肺哥！——我这会儿唯一发愁的，是上哪儿弄够「投射」的料子；这座城一半也喂不饱我。

**费斯**：可不是，先生！买教堂的房顶去吧。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：着啊。

**费斯**：是。让教堂光着头，跟底下听道的会众一样；要不就新给它们戴一顶木瓦帽。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：不，木瓦好是好：木瓦压在椽子上轻，肺哥。——肺哥，我要把你从炉子跟前解放出来；我要还你好气色，普夫（Puffe），那在炉灰里烧掉的好气色；还要修补修补这个脑子——叫金属的烟气熏坏了的。

===Original===
**Face**：I have blown, sir, Hard for your worship; thrown by many a coal, When ’twas not beech; weighed those I put in, just, To keep your heat still even; these bleared eyes Have waked to read your several colours, sir, Of the pale citron, the green lion, the crow, The peacock’s tail, the plumed swan.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：And, lastly, Thou hast descryed the flower, the *sanguis agni*?

**Face**：Yes, sir.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Where’s master?

**Face**：At his prayers, sir, he; Good man, he’s doing his devotions For the success.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Lungs, I will set a period To all thy labours; thou shalt be the master Of my seraglio.

**Face**：Good, sir.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：But do you hear? I’ll geld you, Lungs.

**Face**：Yes, sir.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：For I do mean To have a list of wives and concubines, Equal with Solomon, who had the stone Alike with me; and I will make me a back With the elixir, that shall be as tough As Hercules, to encounter fifty a night.— Thou’rt sure thou saw’st it blood?

**Face**：Both blood and spirit, sir.

===Chinese===
**费斯**：我为您老人家，先生，可是卖力吹过的；多少回煤不对头——不是山毛榉——我就撇下不烧；添进炉的每一块都称过分量，好教您的火候始终平匀；这双熬红的眼睛，先生，守着读过您的各色景象：淡柠檬、绿狮子、乌鸦、孔雀尾，还有插羽的天鹅。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：那么，到末了，你也望见了那朵花，*sanguis agni*（羔羊之血）了？

**费斯**：望见了，先生。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：先生人在哪儿？

**费斯**：在祷告呢，先生，他那位好人；他正为功成的机缘做他的敬拜。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：肺哥，我要给你的苦差画个句号；你要做我后宫的总管。

**费斯**：好，先生。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：可你听着没有？我要把你阉了，肺哥。

**费斯**：是，先生。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：因为我打算蓄一册妻妾，与所罗门旗鼓相当——他当年也有石头，同我一样；我还要用灵药给自己熬出一副腰背，结实得赛赫拉克勒斯，一夜对付五十个。——你拿得准，看见它变血了？

**费斯**：又是血又是灵，先生。

===Original===
**Sir Epicure Mammon**：I will have all my beds blown up, not stuffed; Down is too hard: and then, mine oval room Filled with such pictures as Tiberius took From Elephantis, and dull Aretine But coldly imitated. Then, my glasses Cut in more subtle angles, to disperse And multiply the figures, as I walk Naked between my succubae. My mists I’ll have of perfume, vapoured ’bout the room, To lose ourselves in; and my baths, like pits To fall into; from whence we will come forth, And roll us dry in gossamer and roses.— Is it arrived at ruby?—Where I spy A wealthy citizen, or [a] rich lawyer, Have a sublimed pure wife, unto that fellow I’ll send a thousand pound to be my cuckold.

**Face**：And I shall carry it?

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：No. I’ll have no bawds, But fathers and mothers: they will do it best, Best of all others. And my flatterers Shall be the pure and gravest of divines, That I can get for money. My mere fools, Eloquent burgesses, and then my poets The same that writ so subtly of the fart, Whom I will entertain still for that subject. The few that would give out themselves to be Court and town-stallions, and, each-where, bely Ladies who are known most innocent for them; Those will I beg, to make me eunuchs of: And they shall fan me with ten ostrich tails Apiece, made in a plume to gather wind. We will be brave, Puffe, now we have the medicine. My meat shall all come in, in Indian shells, Dishes of agate set in gold, and studded With emeralds, sapphires, hyacinths, and rubies. The tongues of carps, dormice, and camels’ heels, Boiled in the spirit of Sol, and dissolved pearl, Apicius’ diet, ’gainst the epilepsy: And I will eat these broths with spoons of amber, Headed with diamond and carbuncle. My footboy shall eat pheasants, calvered salmons, Knots, godwits, lampreys: I myself will have The beards of barbels served, instead of salads; Oiled mushrooms; and the swelling unctuous paps Of a fat pregnant sow, newly cut off, Dressed with an exquisite, and poignant sauce; For which, I’ll say unto my cook, “There’s gold, Go forth, and be a knight.”

===Chinese===
**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：我所有的床都要用气吹起来，不用填絮；鸭绒太硬：然后，我那间椭圆的屋子，要挂满提比略当年从埃勒凡蒂斯（Elephantis）手里没收来的那些画，乏味的阿雷蒂诺（Aretine）不过冷冰冰地仿了个皮毛。然后，我的镜子要磨出更刁钻的棱角，把人影分散又翻倍，好教我赤条条地穿行在众魔女（succubae）之间。我的香雾，要用香水蒸满一屋，好教我们把彼此都走丢；我的浴池，要深得像坑，一跤跌进去；从那儿出来，我们就地一滚，在游丝和玫瑰里滚干。——到红宝石了没有？——哪里让我瞧见一个阔气的市民，或者有钱的律师，娶着一位升华提纯、纯而又纯的太太，我就给那家伙送上一千镑，求他赏脸做我的乌龟。

**费斯**：这差由我来跑？

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：不。我不用鸨母，只用爹和娘：他们最拿手，比谁都在行。我的谄媚团，要全是我花钱请得来的最纯粹、最庄严的神学家。我的纯牌傻瓜，要能言善辩的议员老爷；再有我的诗人们——就是把「屁」写得那样精微的那一位，我要长年养着他，专攻这个题目。那少数几个自封宫廷与城中「种马」、到处污蔑明明清白的女人们的，我要乞求他们来给我当阉人：他们一人拿十根鸵鸟尾羽，扎成一柄大羽扇给我扇风。咱们要抖起来了，普夫，如今咱们有了药。我的肉食都要盛在印度贝壳里端上来，玛瑙盘子镶着金，嵌满祖母绿、蓝宝石、红锆玉和红宝石。鲤鱼舌、睡鼠、驼蹄，拿「太阳之精」和溶化的珍珠煮了——阿皮奇乌斯（Apicius）的食谱，专防羊痫风：这些羹汤，我要用琥珀匙来吃，匙头镶钻石、缀红玉。我的小马夫要吃野鸡、腌鲑鱼片、鸻鸟、塍鹬、七鳃鳗：我本人呢，上鲃鱼须，当沙拉；香油浸的蘑菇；还有一头肥硕怀胎的母猪新割下来的、饱满多油的奶头，浇一味精美刺激的浓汁；为这个，我要对厨子说：「拿金子去，出去，当你的骑士去。」

===Original===
**Face**：Sir, I’ll go look A little, how it heightens.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Do.—My shirts I’ll have of taffeta-sarsnet, soft and light As cobwebs; and for all my other raiment, It shall be such as might provoke the Persian, Were he to teach the world riot anew. My gloves of fishes’ and birds’ skins, perfumed With gums of paradise, and eastern air—

**Pertinax Surly**：And do you think to have the stone with this?

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：No, I do think t’ have all this with the stone.

**Pertinax Surly**：Why, I have heard he must be *homo frugi*, A pious, holy, and religious man, One free from mortal sin, a very virgin.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：That makes it, sir; he is so: but I buy it; My venture brings it me. He, honest wretch, A notable, superstitious, good soul, Has worn his knees bare, and his slippers bald, With prayer and fasting for it: and, sir, let him Do it alone, for me, still. Here he comes. Not a profane word afore him: ’tis poison.—

===Chinese===
**费斯**：先生，我去瞧一眼，它升得怎么样了。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：去。——我的衬衫，要用塔夫绸软缎做，软而且轻，赛过蛛网；至于我其余的行头，得花哨到能挑动波斯王——哪怕他要重新教全世界荒淫一遍。我的手套用鱼皮鸟皮做，熏上天堂的树胶与东方的香风——

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：您以为，靠这些就能得着石头？

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：不，我以为，靠石头就能得着这一切。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：嘿，我听说得石头的人必须是 *homo frugi*（节俭自持之人），一个虔诚、圣洁、敬神的人，一个不犯死罪的人，一个道地的童身。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：正因如此，先生；他正是那样的人：可石头是我买的；我的股份替我挣来它。他，那可怜人，一个出了名的迷信的好灵魂，为了它祷告斋戒，膝盖跪秃了，鞋底磨穿了：那么，先生，就让他独自个儿一直虔下去吧，与我无干。他来了。当着他，一个字也别胡说：那是毒药。—

===Original===
**Subtle**：Gentle son, good morrow, And to your friend there. What is he, is with you?

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：An heretic, that I did bring along, In hope, sir, to convert him.

**Subtle**：Son, I doubt You are covetous, that thus you meet your time In the just point: prevent your day at morning. This argues something, worthy of a fear Of importune and carnal appetite. Take heed you do not cause the blessing leave you, With your ungoverned haste. I should be sorry To see my labours, now even at perfection, Got by long watching and large patience, Not prosper where my love and zeal hath placed them. Which (heaven I call to witness, with yourself, To whom I have poured my thoughts) in all my ends, Have looked no way, but unto public good, To pious uses, and dear charity Now grown a prodigy with men. Wherein If you, my son, should now prevaricate, And, to your own particular lusts employ So great and catholic a bliss, be sure A curse will follow, yea, and overtake Your subtle and most secret ways.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：I know, sir; You shall not need to fear me; I but come, To have you confute this gentleman.

**Pertinax Surly**：Who is, Indeed, sir, somewhat costive of belief Toward your stone; would not be gulled.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：温柔的儿子，早安，也问您那位朋友安。同您一道的是谁？

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：一个异端，先生，我随身带来的，指望您感化他。

**萨特尔**：儿子，我怕您起了贪心，才这样掐着正点赶您的时辰：日头刚上就抢了您的日子来。这可有些说头，透着一股叫人担心的、急切而肉欲的口腹之念。小心别让您这不加管束的性急，把祝福赶跑了。我的劳作，如今正到了功德圆满的关口，是长夜守望与宽大忍耐挣来的，若在我以爱与热忱安放它的地方不得昌盛，我可是要伤心的。（我叫上天作证，也叫您本人作证——我的心思尽数向您倾倒过的——）我的一切目标，从不看别处，只看公益，看敬虔的用途，看如今世人眼中已成奇观的亲爱慈善。倘若您，我的儿子，这时节背信反悔，把这样伟大而普世的福分拿去填一己的私欲，您放心，咒诅必随后跟上，而且要追上您最细最密的曲折门路。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：我知道，先生；您不必提防我；我来，不过是请您驳倒这位先生。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：这位先生呢，说老实话，对您的石头，信念有些便秘；不肯上当。

===Original===
**Subtle**：Well, son, All that I can convince him in, is this, The work is done, bright Sol is in his robe. We have a medicine of the triple soul, The glorified spirit. Thanks be to heaven, And make us worthy of it!—Ulen Spiegel!

**Face**：*（Within.）* Anon, sir.

**Subtle**：Look well to the register. And let your heat still lessen by degrees, To the aludels.

**Face**：*（Within.）* Yes, sir.

**Subtle**：Did you look On the bolt’s head yet?

**Face**：*（Within.）* Which? On D, sir?

**Subtle**：Ay; What’s the complexion?

**Face**：*（Within.）* Whitish.

**Subtle**：Infuse vinegar, To draw his volatile substance and his tincture: And let the water in glass E be filtered, And put into the gripe’s egg. Lute him well; And leave him closed in balneo.

**Face**：*（Within.）* I will, sir.

**Pertinax Surly**：What a brave language here is! Next to canting.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：那么，儿子，我能叫他折服的只有这一条：功已成了一半，明亮的太阳穿上了他的袍子。我们得了三重灵魂之药，得了那荣光之灵。感谢上苍，也叫我们配得上它！——乌伦·施皮格尔（Ulen Spiegel）！

**费斯**：*（幕内。）*来了，先生。

**萨特尔**：照看好记录簿。让您的火力仍按次第递减，减到阿鲁德尔罐（aludel）那一档。

**费斯**：*（幕内。）*是，先生。

**萨特尔**：长颈瓶您瞧过了没有？

**费斯**：*（幕内。）*哪一只？D 号吗，先生？

**萨特尔**：对；气色如何？

**费斯**：*（幕内。）*发白。

**萨特尔**：注进醋，把它的挥发性子和染色素引出来：E 号瓶里的水要过滤，倒进鹫蛋瓶（gripe's egg）里。用泥封把它封严；让它闭在浴锅（balneo）里。

**费斯**：*（幕内。）*就办，先生。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：好一门神气的语言！赛过黑话了。

===Original===
**Subtle**：I have another work, you never saw, son, That three days since past the philosopher’s wheel, In the lent heat of Athanor; and’s become Sulphur of Nature.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：But ’tis for me?

**Subtle**：What need you? You have enough in that is perfect.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：O but—

**Subtle**：Why, this is covetise!

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：No, I assure you, I shall employ it all in pious uses, Founding of colleges and grammar schools, Marrying young virgins, building hospitals, And now and then a church.

**Subtle**：How now!

**Face**：Sir, please you, Shall I not change the filter?

**Subtle**：Marry, yes; And bring me the complexion of glass B.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Have you another?

**Subtle**：Yes, son; were I assured— Your piety were firm, we would not want The means to glorify it: but I hope the best.— I mean to tinct C in sand-heat tomorrow, And give him imbibition.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：我还有一件功，儿子，您从没见过：三天前过了哲人之轮，在阿塔诺炉（Athanor）的文火里；已然成了「自然之硫」。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：可那是给我的？

**萨特尔**：您要它做什么？您手里完善的份已经尽够了。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：哦，可是——

**萨特尔**：怎么，这就是贪心！

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：不，我向您担保，我要把它尽数用于敬虔的事业：兴办学院和文法学校，给年轻处女们配婚，盖医院，间或盖一座教堂。

**萨特尔**：怎么说！

**费斯**：先生，您吩咐，滤纸要不要换？

**萨特尔**：正好，换吧；再把 B 号瓶的气色取来我看。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：您还有一件？

**萨特尔**：是，儿子；只要我拿得稳——您的敬虔坚定不移，咱们就不愁没有荣耀它的法子：不过我往好里盼。——我打算明天把 C 放进沙浴里染色，再给它一回灌注（imbibition）。

===Original===
**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Of white oil?

**Subtle**：No, sir, of red. F is come over the helm too, I thank my Maker, in St. Mary’s bath, And shows *lac virginis*. Blessed be heaven! I sent you of his faeces there calcined: Out of that calx, I have won the salt of mercury.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：By pouring on your rectified water?

**Subtle**：Yes, and reverberating in Athanor.

**Face**：The ground black, sir.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：That’s your crow’s head?

**Pertinax Surly**：Your cockscomb’s, is it not?

**Subtle**：No, ’tis not perfect. Would it were the crow! That work wants something.

**Pertinax Surly**：*（Aside.）* O, I looked for this. The hay’s a pitching.

**Subtle**：Are you sure you loosed them In their own menstrue?

**Face**：Yes, sir, and then married them, And put them in a bolt’s head nipped to digestion, According as you bade me, when I set The liquor of Mars to circulation In the same heat.

**Subtle**：The process then was right.

**Face**：Yes, by the token, sir, the retort brake, And what was saved was put into the pelican, And signed with Hermes’ seal.

**Subtle**：I think ’twas so. We should have a new amalgama.

**Pertinax Surly**：*（Aside.）* O, this ferret Is rank as any polecat.

**Subtle**：But I care not: Let him e’en die; we have enough beside, In embrion. H has his white shirt on?

**Face**：Yes, sir, He’s ripe for inceration, he stands warm, In his ash-fire. I would not you should let Any die now, if I might counsel, sir, For luck’s sake to the rest: it is not good.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：He says right.

**Pertinax Surly**：*（Aside.）* Ay, are you bolted?

**Face**：Nay, I know’t, sir, I have seen the ill fortune. What is some three ounces Of fresh materials?

===Chinese===
**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：用白油？

**萨特尔**：不，先生，用红的。F 也过了蒸馏盔了，感谢造物主，在圣玛丽浴里，现出 *lac virginis*（童女乳）。上天保佑！那回送去给您看的它的渣滓煅灰：从那份灰里，我已赢得了汞之盐。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：靠浇上您的精馏水？

**萨特尔**：是，再在阿塔诺炉里返焰煅烧。

**费斯**：底子发黑了，先生。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：那就是您的「乌鸦头」了？

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：是您那「鸡冠」吧，可不是？

**萨特尔**：不，还不完善。但愿它就是乌鸦！那一件还欠着火候。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：*（旁白。）*哦，我就等着这个呢。干草又叉起来了。

**萨特尔**：您拿得准是在它们自家的溶剂里解离的？

**费斯**：是，先生，解离完就给它们行了婚礼，装进长颈瓶，掐着火候送进消化，全照您吩咐的；那时我正把火星液放在同一火候里循环。

**萨特尔**：那么工序是不错的。

**费斯**：是，有个凭证，先生：曲颈瓶炸了，抢出来的都挪进了鹈鹕瓶（pelican），用赫尔墨斯之印封了口。

**萨特尔**：我想是这样。咱们该重新配一份汞合金（amalgama）。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：*（旁白。）*哦，这条雪貂，骚得赛过臭鼬。

**萨特尔**：不过我不心疼：由它死去吧；咱们旁的尽有，还都在胚胎里。H 穿上白衬衫了没有？

**费斯**：穿上了，先生；蜡化（inceration）的火候正熟，它站在灰火里，暖暖的。要是我说得上话，先生，这会儿您一条也别叫它死——为了其余的运气：不吉利的。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：他说得对。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：*（旁白。）*嗬，你们这就躲进洞了？

**费斯**：不，我知道底细，先生，我见过那霉运。三几盎司新料算什么？

===Original===
**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Is’t no more?

**Face**：No more, sir. Of gold, t’amalgam with some six of mercury.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Away, here’s money. What will serve?

**Face**：Ask him, sir.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：How much?

**Subtle**：Give him nine pound:—you may give him ten.

**Pertinax Surly**：Yes, twenty, and be cozened, do.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：There ’tis. *（Gives **Face** the money.）*

**Subtle**：This needs not; but that you will have it so, To see conclusions of all: for two Of our inferior works are at fixation, A third is in ascension. Go your ways. Have you set the oil of luna in kemia?

**Face**：Yes, sir.

**Subtle**：And the philosopher’s vinegar?

**Face**：Ay.

**Pertinax Surly**：We shall have a salad!

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：When do you make projection?

**Subtle**：Son, be not hasty, I exalt our medicine, By hanging him in *balneo vaporoso*, And giving him solution; then congeal him; And then dissolve him; then again congeal him; For look, how oft I iterate the work, So many times I add unto his virtue. As, if at first one ounce convert a hundred, After his second loose, he’ll turn a thousand; His third solution, ten; his fourth, a hundred: After his fifth, a thousand thousand ounces Of any imperfect metal, into pure Silver or gold, in all examinations, As good as any of the natural mine. Get you your stuff here against afternoon, Your brass, your pewter, and your andirons.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Not those of iron?

**Subtle**：Yes, you may bring them too: We’ll change all metals.

**Pertinax Surly**：I believe you in that.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Then I may send my spits?

**Subtle**：Yes, and your racks.

**Pertinax Surly**：And dripping-pans, and pot-hangers, and hooks? Shall he not?

**Subtle**：If he please.

**Pertinax Surly**：—To be an ass.

**Subtle**：How, sir!

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：This gentleman you must bear withal: I told you he had no faith.

**Pertinax Surly**：And little hope, sir; But much less charity, should I gull myself.

===Chinese===
**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：就这点儿？

**费斯**：就这点儿，先生。金子，拿来同六份汞合成汞合金。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：拿去，钱在这儿。要多少才够？

**费斯**：问他，先生。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：多少？

**萨特尔**：给他九镑：——您给十镑也使得。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：是啊，二十镑，挨一回宰，快着呢。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：拿去。*（把钱交给**费斯**。）*

**萨特尔**：这本用不着；只是您非要看到个水落石出：咱们两件低层的功已经到了固定，第三件正在升腾。去吧。月油您放进「凯米亚」（kemia，封釜）了没有？

**费斯**：放了，先生。

**萨特尔**：哲人醋呢？

**费斯**：放了。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：咱们要拌沙拉吃了！

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：几时投射？

**萨特尔**：儿子，不要性急，我正把咱们的药「高举」：把它悬在 *balneo vaporoso*（蒸气浴）里，给它解离；再让它凝结；再溶解；再凝结一回；要知道，这功我每重演一遍，就给它添一分德性。好比起初一盎司化一百；二度松解之后，它就化一千；三度解离，化万；四度，化十万：五度过后，十万万盎司——随便什么贱金属，尽数化作纯银或纯金，任凭怎样查验，同天然矿里出的成色一样。您下午就把料送到这儿来：您的铜器、锡器，还有您的柴架（andirons）。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：铁的不要？

**萨特尔**：要，那种也一并带来：咱们诸金并变。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：这个我信您。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：那我可以把烤肉叉也送来？

**萨特尔**：可以，还有您的烤架。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：还有接油盘、挂锅钩、吊火钩？是不是，啊？

**萨特尔**：他乐意就成。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：——好去当一头驴。

**萨特尔**：什么话，先生！

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：这位先生您得担待：我说过，他没有信心。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：望德也不多，先生；至于爱德，更是少得可怜——若要我自己骗自己。

===Original===
**Subtle**：Why, what have you observed, sir, in our art, Seems so impossible?

**Pertinax Surly**：But your whole work, no more. That you should hatch gold in a furnace, sir, As they do eggs in Egypt!

**Subtle**：Sir, do you Believe that eggs are hatched so?

**Pertinax Surly**：If I should?

**Subtle**：Why, I think that the greater miracle. No egg but differs from a chicken more Than metals in themselves.

**Pertinax Surly**：That cannot be. The egg’s ordained by nature to that end, And is a chicken *in potentia*.

**Subtle**：The same we say of lead and other metals, Which would be gold, if they had time.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：And that Our art doth further.

**Subtle**：Ay, for ’twere absurb To think that nature in the earth bred gold Perfect in the instant: something went before. There must be remote matter.

**Pertinax Surly**：Ay, what is that?

**Subtle**：Marry, we say—

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Ay, now it heats: stand, Father, Pound him to dust.

**Subtle**：It is, of the one part, A humid exhalation, which we call *Materia liquida*, or the unctuous water; On the other part, a certain crass and vicious Portion of earth; both which, concorporate, Do make the elementary matter of gold; Which is not yet *propria materia*, But common to all metals and all stones; For, where it is forsaken of that moisture, And hath more dryness, it becomes a stone: Where it retains more of the humid fatness, It turns to sulphur, or to quicksilver, Who are the parents of all other metals. Nor can this remote matter suddenly Progress so from extreme unto extreme, As to grow gold, and leap o’er all the means. Nature doth first beget the imperfect, then Proceeds she to the perfect. Of that airy And oily water, mercury is engendered; Sulphur of the fat and earthy part; the one, Which is the last, supplying the place of male, The other of the female, in all metals. Some do believe hermaphrodeity, That both do act and suffer. But these two Make the rest ductile, malleable, extensive. And even in gold they are; for we do find Seeds of them, by our fire, and gold in them; And can produce the species of each metal More perfect thence, than nature doth in earth. Beside, who doth not see in daily practice Art can beget bees, hornets, beetles, wasps, Out of the carcases and dung of creatures; Yea, scorpions of an herb, being rightly placed? And these are living creatures, far more perfect And excellent than metals.

===Chinese===
**萨特尔**：哦，您在我们的艺术里，先生，看出什么这样办不到的事？

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：您那整件的功，没别的。你们竟能在炉子里孵出金子来，先生，像埃及人孵蛋一样！

**萨特尔**：先生，您信不信，蛋真是那样孵出来的？

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：要是我信呢？

**萨特尔**：嘿，那我看这才是更大的奇迹。一枚蛋同一只雏鸡的差别，比诸金属彼此间的差别，不知要大多少。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：那可说不通。蛋是自然指定了奔那个结局去的，本就是一只*in potentia*（潜在）的雏鸡。

**萨特尔**：铅和其他金属，我们也是这么说：只要给它们时间，它们都要变成金子。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：而这，正是咱们的艺术给它们的助力。

**萨特尔**：是，因为若说自然在土里一刹那间就孕育出完善的金子，那才荒唐：总有在前头的东西。必得有「先行质料」（remote matter）。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：哦，那是什么？

**萨特尔**：这个嘛，我们说——

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：对，这下热起来了：站住，神父，把他捣成齑粉。

**萨特尔**：它一半是，一种湿润的呵气，我们唤作 *Materia liquida*（液态质料），或叫油润之水；另一半是，土里一份粗重顽劣的成分；两者掺合一体，便构成金的元素质料；这还不是*propria materia*（专属质料），而是一切金属、一切石头所共有的；哪里被那份湿气舍了，干性多了，它就成石头：哪里多留着湿润的膏脂，它就变成硫，或变成水银——它们是其余一切金属的父母。再者，这先行质料也没法子一蹴而就地从一个极端跨到另一个极端，一步登天变成金子，把中间的阶梯全跳过去。自然先孕育不完善的，然后才行到完善的。从那又气又油的水里，生出汞；从那膏腴的土性里，生出硫；后者居末，在一切金属里充当雄的角色，前者充当雌的。也有人信它们雌雄同体（hermaphrodeity），两样都施也两样都受。可正是这两样，教其余金属能延展、能锤锻、能拉长。就连金子里也有它们；因为借我们的火，我们从金子里找得见它们的种子，也从它们里头找得见金；还能从那里产出每一种金属的「种」，比自然在土里产的更完善。再说，谁在日常经验里看不见：技艺能从死尸和粪土里生出蜜蜂、马蜂、甲虫、黄蜂；一株香草，放对了地方，还能生出蝎子？而这些是活物，比金属可完善、可贵重得多了。

===Original===
**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Well said, Father! Nay, if he take you in hand, sir, with an argument, He’ll bray you in a mortar.

**Pertinax Surly**：Pray you, sir, stay. Rather than I’ll be brayed, sir, I’ll believe That Alchemy is a pretty kind of game, Somewhat like tricks o’ the cards, to cheat a man With charming.

**Subtle**：Sir?

**Pertinax Surly**：What else are all your terms, Whereon no one of your writers ’grees with other? Of your elixir, your *lac virginis*, Your stone, your medicine, and your chrysosperm, Your sal, your sulphur, and your mercury, Your oil of height, your tree of life, your blood, Your marcasite, your tutie, your magnesia, Your toad, your crow, your dragon, and your panther; Your sun, your moon, your firmament, your adrop, Your lato, azoch, zernich, chibrit, heautarit, And then your red man, and your white woman, With all your broths, your menstrues, and materials, Of piss and eggshells, women’s terms, man’s blood, Hair o’ the head, burnt clouts, chalk, merds, and clay, Powder of bones, scalings of iron, glass, And worlds of other strange ingredients, Would burst a man to name?

**Subtle**：And all these named, Intending but one thing; which art our writers Used to obscure their art.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Sir, so I told him— Because the simple idiot should not learn it, And make it vulgar.

**Subtle**：Was not all the knowledge Of the Egyptians writ in mystic symbols? Speak not the scriptures oft in parables? Are not the choicest fables of the poets, That were the fountains and first springs of wisdom, Wrapped in perplexed allegories?

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：I urged that, And cleared to him, that Sisyphus was damned To roll the ceaseless stone, only because He would have made Ours common.

===Chinese===
**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：说得好，神父！嗐，先生，他要是拿论证把你抓上手，他能把你搁进臼里捣烂。

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：请您了，先生，且慢。与其进臼受捣，先生，我宁可相信：炼金术是种蛮漂亮的游戏，有点像纸牌戏法，靠念咒把人哄了。

**萨特尔**：什么？

**珀蒂纳克斯·瑟利**：不然，你们那套名目还算什么？你们的写书人，没一个跟另一个说得拢：你们的灵药、你们的 *lac virginis*（童女乳）、你们的石头、你们的药、你们的金种（chrysosperm），你们的盐、你们的硫、你们的汞，你们的至高之油、生命树、血，你们的白铁矿（marcasite）、锌华（tutie）、磁土（magnesia），你们的蟾蜍、乌鸦、龙、豹；你们的太阳、月亮、苍穹、锑乳（adrop），你们的黄铜（lato）、水银精（azoch）、砒（zernich）、锑盐（chibrit）、汞硫合剂（heautarit），还有你们的红男人、白女人，连同你们那一切汤液、溶剂与原料——尿和蛋壳、女人的月信、男人的血、头发、烧布片、白垩、粪、黏土、骨粉、铁屑、玻璃，还有一世界念出口就能把人撑破的怪名目，算什么？

**萨特尔**：这些名目尽管千百，说的只是一物；我们的写书人正是用它来遮掩这门艺术。

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：先生，我就是这么跟他说的——免得蠢货俗人学了去，把它弄成大路货。

**萨特尔**：埃及人的一切学问，不都写在神秘符文里？圣书不也常常用比喻说话？诗人们那些最精粹的寓言——那可是智慧的泉源和头一股活水——不都裹在费解的谜语里？

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：这一点是我掰开揉碎讲给他的：西西弗之所以被罚，去滚那块永不停歇的石头，只因为他想把咱们的「这一块」公之于众。

===Original===
**Dol Common**：*（Appears at the door.）*— Who is this?

**Subtle**：’Sprecious!—What do you mean? Go in, good lady, Let me entreat you.

**Face**：Sir.

**Subtle**：You very knave! Do you use me thus?

**Face**：Wherein, sir?

**Subtle**：Go in and see, you traitor. Go!

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Who is it, sir?

**Subtle**：Nothing, sir; nothing.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：What’s the matter, good sir? I have not seen you thus distempered: who is’t?

**Subtle**：All arts have still had, sir, their adversaries; But ours the most ignorant.—

**Face**：’Twas not my fault, sir; she would speak with you.

**Subtle**：Would she, sir! Follow me.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：*（Stopping him.）* Stay, Lungs.

**Face**：I dare not, sir.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：Stay, man; what is she?

**Face**：A lord’s sister, sir.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：How! Pray thee, stay.

**Face**：She’s mad, sir, and sent hither— He’ll be mad too.—

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：I warrant thee.— Why sent hither?

**Face**：Sir, to be cured.

**Subtle**：*（Within.）* Why, rascal!

**Face**：Lo you!—Here, sir!

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：’Fore God, a Bradamante, a brave piece.

**Pertinax Surly**：Heart, this is a bawdyhouse! I will be burnt else.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：O, by this light, no: do not wrong him. He’s Too scrupulous that way: it is his vice. No, he’s a rare physician, do him right, An excellent Paracelsian, and has done Strange cures with mineral physic. He deals all With spirits, he; he will not hear a word Of Galen; or his tedious recipes.—

**Face**：Softly, sir; speak softly. I meant To have told your worship all. This must not hear.

**Sir Epicure Mammon**：No, he will not be “gulled;” let him alone.

**Face**：You are very right, sir, she is a most rare scholar, And is gone mad with studying Broughton’s works. If you but name a word touching the Hebrew, She falls into her fit, and will discourse So learnedly of genealogies, As you would run mad too, to hear her, sir.

===Chinese===
**多尔·康门**：*（在门口现身。）*——这是谁？

**萨特尔**：『老天爷的』！——您这是什么意思？进去，好夫人，求您了。

**费斯**：先生。

**萨特尔**：你这个刁棍！就这样对付我？

**费斯**：哪儿的话，先生？

**萨特尔**：进去瞧瞧去，你这奸贼。去！

**伊壁鸠鲁·玛蒙爵士**：是谁，先生？

**萨特尔**：没谁，先生；没什么。
